## Q13. For UT(3,ℤ), take S={I±E_(12),I±E_(23)}. If c_(n) counts length-n words over S e…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 1

For UT(3,ℤ), take S={I±E_(12),I±E_(23)}. If c_(n) counts length-n words over S equal to the identity, is ∑\_(n≥0) c_(n) t^(n) non-D-algebraic (satisfying no nonzero polynomial differential equation)?

**Context.** Origin: The cogrowth questions are joint with Igor Pak. The quiver and sign-imbalance questions arise from Soukup’s own papers incorporated into the thesis.

**Source.** David Martin Soukup. *Computational Complexity in Combinatorics and Algebra*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2024. Advisor(s): Igor Pak. [primary source](https://www.math.ucla.edu/~pak/papers/Soukup-thesis.pdf) · [record](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/1dm9g64v) Location: §1.6.3, printed p.36. Search-qualified status: Bodart (2024) proves non-D-finiteness for a virtually Heisenberg example but leaves the discrete Heisenberg case as a further question (§4.2). No resolution of this stronger statement found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13395) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.09419)

## Q47. On graphs of maximum degree d, can a Markov-chain algorithm initialized from the…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.1.13) · **Collection** 1

On graphs of maximum degree d, can a Markov-chain algorithm initialized from the semidefinite-programming solution efficiently attain approximation ratio α\_(GW)+Ω(d^(−1/2)) for Max-Cut, where α\_(GW)≈0.87856 is the Goemans–Williamson ratio?

**Context.** Origin: Both questions arise from joint work with Sidhanth Mohanty, Amit Rajaraman and David X. Wu.

**Source.** Brice Huang. *Statistical and Algorithmic Thresholds in Spin Glasses*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2025. Advisor(s): Guy Bresler; Nike Sun. **Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09075) · [2](https://www.bricehuang.com/index.html)

## Q48. Let H_(N) be a centered Gaussian Hamiltonian on {σ∈ℝ^(N):∥σ∥^(2)=N} with covaria…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.1.14) · **Collection** 1

Let H_(N) be a centered Gaussian Hamiltonian on {σ∈ℝ^(N):∥σ∥^(2)=N} with covariance 𝔼[H_(N)(σ)H_(N)(τ)]=Nξ(⟨σ,τ⟩/N), where ξ(q)=∑\_(p=2)^(p_(\*)) γ\_(p)^(2) q^(p). Does annealed Langevin dynamics sample its Gibbs measure in polynomial time with vanishing total-variation error whenever ξ^(′)(q)&lt;q/(1−q) for every 0&lt;q<1? Temperature is absorbed into ξ.

**Context.** Origin: Both questions arise from joint work with Sidhanth Mohanty, Amit Rajaraman and David X. Wu.

**Source.** Brice Huang. *Statistical and Algorithmic Thresholds in Spin Glasses*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2025. Advisor(s): Guy Bresler; Nike Sun. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/6aabbc9a-661d-4f61-8389-c91f797c7d20/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/6aabbc9a-661d-4f61-8389-c91f797c7d20/content#page=1) Location: §7.1.5, Problem 7.1.14, p.376; model and threshold pp.369–372. Search-qualified status: The associated paper and 2025 talk leave this open. July 2026 work gives warm-start Glauber guarantees for SK at β<1/2, not the full spherical non-shattering regime.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09075) · [2](https://www.bricehuang.com/files/slides/wpi.pdf) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08160)


<a id="q54"></a>

## Q54. For the Gaussian model of Definition 5.3.4, can restarted PDHG identify the opti…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 1

For the Gaussian model of Definition 5.3.4, can restarted PDHG identify the optimal basis in tilde(O)(n^(3/2)/δ) iterations with probability at least 1−δ for non-exponentially-small δ? The LP is min bar(c)^(T)x subject to Ax=A hat(x), x≥0, with A∈ℝ^(m×n) having independent N(0,1) entries, hat(x)=(u_(1),0_(n−m)), hat(s)=(0_(m),u_(2)), and bar(c) the projection of hat(s) onto ker A. Independently of A, the coordinates of u=(u_(1),u_(2)) are independent nonnegative sub-Gaussians with densities at most one and bounded distributional parameters. The experiments use folded Gaussians and m=n/2.

**Context.** Origin: Xiong proposes this rate from the dissertation’s random-linear-programming experiments.

**Source.** Zikai Xiong. *New Theory and New Practical Methods for Solving Large-Scale Linear and Conic Optimization*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2025. Advisor(s): Robert M. Freund. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/fb10c17d-9822-4423-acfd-e0b53fef979f/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/fb10c17d-9822-4423-acfd-e0b53fef979f/content#page=1) Location: §5.6.2, p.235; Definition 5.3.4, pp.210–211. Follow-on evidence: Retained explicitly in §6.2 of the September 9, 2026 v4 revision of the associated paper, accepted by Mathematics of Operations Research.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00728) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.00728v4#S6.SS2)

## Q59. Does random restriction yield a comparator-circuit gate-count shrinkage exponent…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 1

Does random restriction yield a comparator-circuit gate-count shrinkage exponent Γ>1/2? Comparator gates send (x,y) to (x∧y,x∨y), with literal-labelled input wires and a designated output; the target concerns gate count rather than wire count.

**Context.** Origin: The comparator-circuit questions arise from work with Zhenjian Lu; the constraint-language question extends joint work with Igor Oliveira.

**Source.** Bruno Pasqualotto Cavalar. *Complexity Theory of Classical and Quantum Computational Devices*. University of Warwick, 2024. Advisor(s): Igor Carboni Oliveira. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/187775/1/WRAP_Theses_Cavalar_2024.pdf) · [record](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/187775/) Location: §6.1, p.119. Search-qualified status: Thesis leaves this open; targeted searches through the check date found no resolution. The 2023 joint paper states the same gap. This is a search-qualified open status, not proof of nonexistence of a solution.

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00453-022-01091-y)

Is Temporal Firefighter fixed-parameter tractable in ψ+Δ? Here ψ is the maximum number of edge lifespans overlapping a time, Δ is underlying maximum degree, and one may permanently defend one unburned vertex before each temporal fire-spreading step to save a target number of vertices.

## Q137. Does every search problem in search-RL have a pseudodeterministic randomized log…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.5.1) · **Collection** 2

Does every search problem in search-RL have a pseudodeterministic randomized logarithmic-space transducer? Here the relation R has polynomially bounded witnesses verifiable with deterministic logarithmic workspace, two-way input access and one-way witness access; membership in search-RL means some randomized log-space transducer finds a valid witness with inverse-polynomial probability on every valid input. Pseudodeterminism asks for a fixed valid witness y_x for each input x that the new transducer outputs with probability at least 2/3, using fresh one-way random bits and without storing an auxiliary reproducibility seed.

**Context.** Origin: Chapter 3 is Grossman’s joint work with Yang P. Liu, published at SODA 2019. Its discussion originates the retained question, explicitly reproduced as a numbered thesis problem.

**Source.** Ofer Grossman. *Pseudo-determinism*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Shafi Goldwasser. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/38a8a0f0-395b-467c-a95c-d573a8e7ed8f/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/4615a1c4-0afa-45ed-b901-5730ab48c05b) Location: Problem 3.5.1, p. 48; definitions 3.2.1–3.2.4, p. 28. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The 2023 thesis retains the general question after proving reproducibility with a logarithmic seed and pseudodeterminism for special graph-search problems. Searches for subsequent search-RL/log-space pseudodeterminism results found no general solution; polynomial-time prime construction and general time-bounded pseudodeterminism results concern different resources.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.04025) · [2](https://epubs.siam.org/doi/10.1137/1.9781611975482.38)


<a id="q138"></a>

## Q138. Characterize the relation of search-BPP(log n) to search-BPP and pseudodetermini…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.5.4) · **Collection** 2

Characterize the relation of search-BPP(log n) to search-BPP and pseudodeterministic-BPP. The class search-BPP(log n) consists of polynomial-time randomized search algorithms with bounded error whose complete output random variable has Shannon entropy O(log n) for every input of length n. In particular, can every search-BPP problem be solved with logarithmic output entropy, and does such an entropy bound suffice to obtain a polynomial-time algorithm that outputs one input-dependent canonical valid witness with high probability? These are retained together as the single two-sided class-comparison problem asked in the thesis.

**Context.** Origin: Chapter 3 is Grossman’s joint work with Yang P. Liu, published at SODA 2019. Its discussion originates the retained question, explicitly reproduced as a numbered thesis problem.

**Source.** Ofer Grossman. *Pseudo-determinism*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): Shafi Goldwasser. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/38a8a0f0-395b-467c-a95c-d573a8e7ed8f/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/4615a1c4-0afa-45ed-b901-5730ab48c05b) Location: Problem 3.5.4, p. 48. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The thesis explicitly poses this entropy-based class comparison following its own log-space low-entropy construction. Searches for logarithmic-output-entropy search-BPP and multi-pseudodeterministic completeness found related results but no resolution of the stated general comparison. The source gives no theorem equating these classes.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.04025) · [2](https://epubs.siam.org/doi/10.1137/1.9781611975482.38)

## Q139. One explicitly proposed formulation of the thesis’s #SAT lifting conjecture is t…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 2

One explicitly proposed formulation of the thesis’s #SAT lifting conjecture is the following. Let C be a Boolean circuit class closed under unbounded conjunction and negation. If, for some epsilon>0, the exact number of satisfying assignments of any n-input C-circuit of size at most 2^(n^epsilon) can be computed deterministically in O(2^(n−n^epsilon)) time, must Quasi-NP contain a language with no polynomial-size SYM∘C circuits? Quasi-NP is the union of nondeterministic n^((log n)^k) time classes over constants k; SYM∘C has an arbitrary symmetric Boolean top gate applied to C-subcircuits. This is the Theorem 4.2 version explicitly requested by Conjecture 1; the source labels the overall conjecture informal.

**Context.** Origin: Conjecture 1 belongs to Vyas’s own joint work with Ryan Williams (STACS 2020; journal version 2023), incorporated as Chapter 4. It proposes strengthening known algorithm-to-lower-bound theorems, rather than merely repeating those older theorems.

**Source.** Nikhil Vyas. *Satisfiability Algorithms and Connections between Algorithms and Circuit Lower Bounds*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2023. Advisor(s): R. Ryan Williams. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/79391641-feff-4f9d-a740-d5d78303c780/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/554dc2db-3552-42e5-9f2e-3bf57bf8c3d1) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 62, applied to Theorem 4.2, p. 60; status note §4.7, p. 93. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The journal version and thesis explicitly state that the full symmetric-gate conjecture survives the known sparse-symmetric and MAJ-top advances. Searches for the exact conjecture, Vyas/Williams, SYM and #SAT through the check date found no later general lifting theorem or disproof.

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00224-022-10106-8) · [2](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.STACS.2020.59)

## Q140. Are first-order snapshots optimal for randomized one-pass, adversarial-order Max…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

Are first-order snapshots optimal for randomized one-pass, adversarial-order Max-DICUT estimation using O-tilde(sqrt(n)) bits? A snapshot counts directed edges between classes of vertex bias (outdegree−indegree)/(outdegree+indegree), using fixed thresholds in [−1,1]. Optimize thresholds and compare with all algorithms in that space regime. Approximation α means output αOPT≤v≤OPT with probability ≥2/3, where OPT is the maximum directed-cut edge fraction.

**Context.** Origin: The snapshot question follows joint work with Saxena, Singer, and Sudan; the deterministic question follows the thesis’s randomized algorithms.

**Source.** Santhoshini Velusamy. *Approximability of Constraint Satisfaction Problems in the Streaming Setting*. Harvard University, 2023. Advisor(s): Madhu Sudan. [primary source](https://dash.harvard.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/c21da307-6849-42de-91e9-163823317654/content) · [record](https://nrs.harvard.edu/URN-3:HUL.INSTREPOS:37377430) Location: Chapter 10, first bullet, p. 176; first-order snapshot definition, p. 51. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Singer’s September 2025 primary survey still describes unresolved square-root-space tradeoffs and a stronger lower-bound conjecture near ratio 1/2. The December 2025 general-graph near-half algorithm uses n^(1−Omega_epsilon(1)) space and therefore does not establish the optimal O-tilde(sqrt(n)) threshold. No resolution of the specific snapshot-optimality question was found.

**Further links.** [1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2025/148/download/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22729) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03916)

## Q143. For each fixed q≥3, does some ε\_q>0 permit randomized q-matroid parity in O\*(2^(…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

For each fixed q≥3, does some ε\_q>0 permit randomized q-matroid parity in O\*(2^((q−ε\_q)k)) time? Input: a matrix over a field representing a rank-qk matroid, with columns partitioned into q-element blocks. Decide whether k blocks form a basis; polynomial factors include representation size.

**Context.** Origin: Matroid parity is joint with Eiben and Wahlström; fair matching with Boehmer. The questions extend their thesis-incorporated algorithms. Shared notation: O\*(·) suppresses polynomial input-size factors. In fair matching, every colored left vertex is assigned to one neighboring right vertex; k counts right vertices. At each right vertex, Max-Min bounds the largest minus smallest color count (including absent colors), and MoV the largest minus second-largest, by input tolerance ℓ.

**Source.** Tomohiro Koana. *New Parameterized Algorithms for Matching and Packing Problems*. Technische Universität Berlin, 2024. Advisor(s): Rolf Niedermeier (initial supervisor); Christian Komusiewicz (completion supervisor). Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: TheoretiCS 2025 still poses the exact exponent-improvement question in §4.2 and its conclusion. Searches through October 2026 found faster algorithms for pair parity or delta-matroids and approximation results, none resolving fixed q≥3 block parity at this exponent.

Can Max-Min/MoV Fair Matching be solved in O\*(2^k) time, for k right vertices and an unrestricted number of colors, including the variant requiring every right vertex to receive at least one left vertex?

**Context.** Origin: Matroid parity is joint with Eiben and Wahlström; fair matching with Boehmer. The questions extend their thesis-incorporated algorithms. Shared notation: O\*(·) suppresses polynomial input-size factors. In fair matching, every colored left vertex is assigned to one neighboring right vertex; k counts right vertices. At each right vertex, Max-Min bounds the largest minus smallest color count (including absent colors), and MoV the largest minus second-largest, by input tolerance ℓ.

**Source.** Tomohiro Koana. *New Parameterized Algorithms for Matching and Packing Problems*. Technische Universität Berlin, 2024. Advisor(s): Rolf Niedermeier (initial supervisor); Christian Komusiewicz (completion supervisor). [primary source](https://depositonce.tu-berlin.de/bitstreams/3746e645-4aa2-4b1d-b5cf-a1ae909a690f/download) · [record](https://depositonce.tu-berlin.de/items/aef0203a-6592-49a6-850d-c3dac571ddc6) Location: §5.4, second bullet, p. 69. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The journal version of the authors’ work retains this exact question. The 2026 structural-parameter classification and searches for faster fair-matching algorithms supplied no O\*(2^k) algorithm for this parameter.

**Further links.** [1](https://d-nb.info/1340885212/34) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tcs.2025.115727)

## Q199. Let P_(n) = {X − I_(n)/n : X ≽ 0, tr X = 1} and P_(n) ⊆ S ⊆ (1 + ε)P_(n). For fi…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

Let P_(n) = {X − I_(n)/n : X ≽ 0, tr X = 1} and P_(n) ⊆ S ⊆ (1 + ε)P_(n). For fixed ε > 0 and k = o(n), must log xc_(𝕊₊^(k))(S) = Ω(n/k)? Here xc_(𝕊₊^(k))(S) is the least number r such that S is a linear image of an affine slice of (𝕊₊^(k))^(r). This is the fixed-accuracy asymptotic content of the thesis’s proposed exp (Cn/((1 + ε)k)) bound.

**Context.** Origin: Explicit author conjecture in the dissertation’s future-directions section, strengthening his own small-PSD-block lower bound; related joint work with Parrilo is arXiv:2105.02080. The k=1 case extends a pre-existing polyhedral question, while this record retains the author’s general k-block formulation.

**Source.** Dogyoon Song. *Addressing Missing Data and Scalable Optimization for Data-driven Decision Making*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2021. Advisor(s): Pablo A. Parrilo; Devavrat Shah. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/f7a53355-9663-4188-a66e-9cb066b2ccc3/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/f7a53355-9663-4188-a66e-9cb066b2ccc3/content#page=1) Location: §8.2.2, unnumbered conjecture, printed p. 171; definitions §§6.2.1,7.2.1–7.2.2. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Associated paper proves only exp(C min{sqrt(n),n/k}) at fixed accuracy. Later searches found approximation algorithms and structured-cone results, not this all-lifts lower bound. Fawzi’s original primary abstract confirms exp(c sqrt(n)) for k=1; a secondary abstract drops the square root and must not be read as a resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2105.02080) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1811.09649) · [3](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s12532-025-00275-1)

## Q228. Do one-way functions suffice for dual-key invisible edits? After choosing e befo…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Do one-way functions suffice for dual-key invisible edits? After choosing e before key generation, an adversary must not distinguish receiving sk with oracle Enc_sk(E(·,e)) from receiving an edited key sk_e with oracle Enc_sk(·). The editing algorithm computes sk_e using only the auxiliary key and e. Alternatively, can one prove that this primitive implies public-key encryption?

**Context.** Origin: Joint work with Goldwasser and Wichs, The Edited Truth (TCC 2017), published under the student’s earlier name Saleet Klein. Shared setup for questions 228, 229: Fix any polynomial-time edit function E(m,e) preserving message length. Encryption must be correct and secure against chosen-plaintext attacks. Generation returns an ordinary key sk and an auxiliary editing key. All algorithms are probabilistic polynomial-time; indistinguishability means negligible distinguishing advantage for every polynomial-time adversary as the security parameter grows.

**Source.** Saleet Mossel. *On Deniable Computation and Sublinear Graph Algorithms*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2022. Advisor(s): Shafi (Shafrira) Goldwasser. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/70774b73-13a7-4f8d-8582-20f06ce7c504/content) · [record](https://www.eecs.mit.edu/eecs-events/doctoral-thesis-on-deniable-computation-and-sublinear-graph-algorithms/) Location: §2.2.3, p. 81 and Definition 2.2.15, pp. 81–82 (PDF numbering agrees).

**Further links.** [1](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/714.pdf) · [2](https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/714)

## Q229. Do one-way functions suffice for dual-key deniable edits? After adaptive access …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

Do one-way functions suffice for dual-key deniable edits? After adaptive access to \$\operatorname{Enc}\_{sk}\$, an adversary selects \$m,e\$. It must not distinguish \$(sk,\operatorname{Enc}\_{sk}(E(m,e)))\$ from \$(sk_{c,e},c)\$, where \$c=\operatorname{Enc}\_{sk}(m)\$ and the auxiliary key together with \$c,e\$ produces \$sk_{c,e}\$. Encryption-oracle access continues afterward. Alternatively, does this primitive imply public-key encryption?

**Context.** Origin: Joint work with Goldwasser and Wichs, The Edited Truth (TCC 2017), published under the student’s earlier name Saleet Klein. Shared setup for questions 228, 229: Fix any polynomial-time edit function E(m,e) preserving message length. Encryption must be correct and secure against chosen-plaintext attacks. Generation returns an ordinary key sk and an auxiliary editing key. All algorithms are probabilistic polynomial-time; indistinguishability means negligible distinguishing advantage for every polynomial-time adversary as the security parameter grows.

**Source.** Saleet Mossel. *On Deniable Computation and Sublinear Graph Algorithms*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2022. Advisor(s): Shafi (Shafrira) Goldwasser. Assume one-way functions exist and \$\mathrm{NP}\ne\mathrm{coNP}\$. Must range avoidance lack a deterministic polynomial-time algorithm? One-way functions are efficiently computable functions whose outputs on uniformly random inputs cannot be inverted with non-negligible probability by probabilistic polynomial-time algorithms.

## Q232. For a simple graph G, a pivot step forms a cluster from a selected vertex and al…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 3

For a simple graph G, a pivot step forms a cluster from a selected vertex and all its remaining neighbors, deletes that cluster, and repeats. Let OPT_pivot(G) be the minimum number of disagreements over all pivot orders, where a disagreement is an edge crossing clusters or a nonedge inside one. Can a polynomial-time algorithm choose a pivot order with expected cost at most (3−δ)OPT_pivot(G) for some constant δ > 0, or even attain OPT_pivot(G) exactly?

**Context.** Origin: New question from Fischer–Kipouridis–Klausen–Thorup, STACS 2025, reproduced in Appendix D.

## Q252. For integers 1≤t≤n&lt;m, determine asymptotically sharp bounds on the minimum size …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 3

For integers 1≤t≤n&lt;m, determine asymptotically sharp bounds on the minimum size of a C³\_t sentence distinguishing A_n from A_m, closing the gap between √n/t and O(n/t), uniformly over m>n.

**Context.** Origin: Counting-logic extension from joint work with Turán, preprinted in 2025 and published at CSL 2026; the ordinary first-order special case is older. Setup for question 252: A_n is the ordered set {0,…,n}, with <, successor and named endpoints. C³\_t allows three reusable variables and counting quantifiers ∃≥k, with 1≤k≤t, and their duals. An atom has size 1; negation and each quantifier add 1; conjunction/disjunction add their operands’ sizes. Counting thresholds do not contribute their digit lengths.

**Source.** Grégoire Jean-Philippe Jacques Fournier. *Logic, Learning, and Explanation: Theoretical and Applied Perspectives on Machine Reasoning*. University of Illinois Chicago, 2025. Advisor(s): György Turán. ## Q350. For every nonzero \$P:\\{-1,1\\}^2\to[0,1]\$, is \$\alpha(P)=\beta(P)\$? Write \$P(x,y)…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1.3) · **Collection** 4

For every nonzero \$P:\\{-1,1\\}^2\to[0,1]\$, is \$\alpha(P)=\beta(P)\$? Write \$P(x,y)=a_0+a_1x+a_2y+a_{12}xy\$. Configurations \$\theta=(b_1,b_2,c)\$ satisfy \$1+sb_1+tb_2+stc\ge0\$ for all \$s,t\in\\{-1,1\\}\$. Put \$L(\theta)=a_0+a_1b_1+a_2b_2+a_{12}c\$ and \$r=(c-b_1b_2)/\sqrt{(1-b_1^2)(1-b_2^2)}\$, using \$r=0\$ when undefined. For continuous odd \$R:[-1,1]\to[-1,1]\$, let \$(Z_1,Z_2)\$ be standard Gaussians with correlation \$r\$ and set \$Y_i=-1\$ iff \$Z_i\le\Phi^{-1}((1-R(b_i))/2)\$; \$\Phi\$ is the standard normal cdf. Over finite configuration distributions \$\Theta\$ with \$\mathbb E_\Theta L>0\$, set \$\alpha(P)=\inf_\Theta\sup_R\mathbb E_\Theta\mathbb E[P(Y_1,Y_2)]/\mathbb E_\Theta L\$. Define \$\beta(P)\$ by restricting to \$a_{12}r\ge0\$ throughout the support. Does the infimum stay unchanged?

## Q351. Must one-pass randomized verification of bipartiteness in the best-order streami…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Must one-pass randomized verification of bipartiteness in the best-order streaming model use ω(log n) bits on n-vertex graphs? The verifier knows n and reads each input edge once. An unbounded prover may only permute the edges. Every bipartite graph must have an ordering accepted with probability at least 2/3; every ordering of every non-bipartite graph must be rejected with probability at least 2/3. The ordering is chosen before the verifier’s private random bits. Nanongkai conjectures a superlogarithmic lower bound.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):

## Q352. Does there exist a constant \$c\$ such that inverting \$c\$-local Boolean permutatio…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Does there exist a constant \$c\$ such that inverting \$c\$-local Boolean permutations is outside polynomial time, as Håstad conjectures? The input is a circuit for a bijection \$f:\\{0,1\\}^n\to\\{0,1\\}^n\$ with every output bit depending on at most \$c\$ input bits, together with \$y\in\\{0,1\\}^n\$. Recover \$f^{-1}(y)\$, or equivalently a specified inverse bit, under the bijectivity promise. Such functions form \$\mathsf{NC}^0\$. The conjecture concerns worst-case sequential hardness; the thesis already constructs permutations whose inversion is \$\mathsf P\$-complete.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Joakim Blikstad → Per Austrin (co-supervision): https://blikstad.gitlab.io/thesis/thesis_blikstad.pdf
Per Austrin → Johan Håstad: https://www.csc.kth.se/aktuellt/numero/2008/08.35.pdf
Johan Håstad → Shafi Goldwasser: https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/9c173e20-1734-4be1-b595-c5b401fc6cdc Origin: The thesis explicitly conjectures nonmembership in P after its own stronger P-hardness construction. It distinguishes earlier Boppana–Lagarias parity-hard and Barrington logspace-hard inversion examples. The P-hardness result became Håstad’s 1987 Information Processing Letters paper.

**Source.** Johan Håstad. *Computational Limitations for Small Depth Circuits*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 1986. Advisor(s): Shafi Goldwasser. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/a408c3e5-b50f-47e6-81f4-20e842a3ded5/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/9c173e20-1734-4be1-b595-c5b401fc6cdc) Location: Conclusions, PDF p. 65, final paragraph; §2.5.

**Further links.** [1](https://blikstad.gitlab.io/thesis/thesis_blikstad.pdf) · [2](https://www.csc.kth.se/aktuellt/numero/2008/08.35.pdf) · [3](https://homes.cs.washington.edu/~ruzzo/papers/limits.pdf) · [4](https://www.wisdom.weizmann.ac.il/~bennyap/pubs/nc0-full.pdf) · [5](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/0020019087900536)

*Duplicate of Qc601a0cd4bbfcd3dab6c.*

## Q354. For every fixed integer k ≥ 2, can MAX-k-SAT on n variables be solved in O\*(2^{n…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.7.1) · **Collection** 4

For every fixed integer k ≥ 2, can MAX-k-SAT on n variables be solved in O\*(2^{n(1−1/(k+1))}) time? The task is to maximize the number of satisfied clauses of a Boolean CNF with at most k literals per clause; O\* suppresses polynomial factors in the input length.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Shyan Akmal → R. Ryan Williams: https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/e126fd25-6bf4-4b6a-b2b6-d4ee7ad8bcf3
R. Ryan Williams → Manuel Blum: https://csd.cs.cmu.edu/academics/doctoral/degrees-conferred/r-ryan-williams Origin: Williams’s own thesis conjectures. The polynomial-space question also arose around his earlier work; the finite-field clique hypothesis was later repeated jointly with Virginia Vassilevska Williams.

**Source.** R. Ryan Williams. *Algorithms and Resource Requirements for Fundamental Problems*. Carnegie Mellon University, 2007. Advisor(s): Manuel Blum. [primary source](https://csd.cs.cmu.edu/sites/default/files/phd-thesis/CMU-CS-07-147.pdf) · [record](https://csd.cs.cmu.edu/academics/doctoral/degrees-conferred/r-ryan-williams) Location: Conjecture 6.7.1, printed p. 86 (PDF p. 97); repeated in Chapter 8, printed p. 100.

## Q357. Can a polynomial-size multi-output circuit family G_n: {0,1}^n → {0,1}^{m(n)}, w…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Can a polynomial-size multi-output circuit family G_n: {0,1}^n → {0,1}^{m(n)}, with m(n) > n, be a pseudorandom generator while using at most one NOT gate in the entire circuit? Thus G_n(U_n) must be computationally indistinguishable from U_{m(n)}, where U_j is uniform on j-bit strings.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Bruno Pasqualotto Cavalar → Igor Carboni Oliveira: https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/187775/1/WRAP_Theses_Cavalar_2024.pdf
Igor Carboni Oliveira → Tal Malkin and Rocco A. Servedio: https://www.dcs.warwick.ac.uk/~igorcarb/index.html Origin: These questions arise from Oliveira’s joint TCC 2015 work with Siyao Guo, Tal Malkin, and Alon Rosen. Shared setup for questions 357, 358: Circuits use fan-in-two AND/OR gates and NOT gates. Security means negligible distinguishing advantage for every polynomial-size adversary.

**Source.** Igor Carboni Oliveira. *Unconditional Lower Bounds in Complexity Theory*. Columbia University, 2015. Advisor(s): Tal Malkin; Rocco A. Servedio. [primary source](https://www.dcs.warwick.ac.uk/~igorcarb/documents/papers/PhD-Oliveira.pdf) · [record](https://www.dcs.warwick.ac.uk/~igorcarb/index.html) Location: §6.5, printed p. 147 (PDF p. 157); definitions §6.2.2–6.2.4.

## Q365. Is 1/2 the optimal polynomial-time approximation ratio for maximizing a common c…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Is 1/2 the optimal polynomial-time approximation ratio for maximizing a common circular-label diameter, assuming P ≠ NP? Given n ≥ 3 distinct planar points, place n congruent disks with pairwise disjoint interiors so that each assigned point lies on its disk’s boundary. If d\* is the largest feasible diameter, the conjecture predicts a polynomial-time guarantee d ≥ d\*/2 and rules out every fixed larger guarantee.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Alexander Dobler → Martin Nöllenburg: https://repositum.tuwien.at/handle/20.500.12708/226672?mode=full
Martin Nöllenburg → Alexander Wolff: https://publikationen.bibliothek.kit.edu/1000011456/810876
Alexander Wolff → Frank Wagner: https://webdoc.sub.gwdg.de/ebook/diss/2003/fu-berlin/2002/237/indexe.html Origin: The conclusion explicitly proposes the 1/2 threshold. Chapter 4.2 is the author’s joint work with Tycho Strijk.

**Source.** Alexander Wolff. *Automated Label Placement in Theory and Practice*. Freie Universität Berlin, 1999. Advisor(s): Frank Wagner. [primary source](https://www1.pub.informatik.uni-wuerzburg.de/pub/wolff/pub/w-alptp-99.pdf) · [record](https://webdoc.sub.gwdg.de/ebook/diss/2003/fu-berlin/2002/237/indexe.html) Location: Conclusion, printed p. 132; model and hardness in §4.2, pp. 82–96.

## Q374. Do there exist an absolute constant \$C\$ and a function \$f:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\$…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 4

Do there exist an absolute constant \$C\$ and a function \$f:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\$ such that every finite undirected capacitated graph \$G\$ with a \$k\$-vertex terminal set \$K\$ admits an undirected capacitated graph \$H\$ containing \$K\$, with at most \$f(k)\$ additional vertices, for which \$\operatorname{cong}\_H(d)\le\operatorname{cong}\_G(d)\le C\operatorname{cong}\_H(d)\$ for every nonnegative multicommodity demand vector \$d\$ on terminal pairs? Here \$\operatorname{cong}\$ is minimum fractional-routing congestion, the maximum edge-load/capacity ratio. No contraction restriction is imposed on \$H\$.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Noah Golowich → Ankur Moitra: https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/093f570f-8544-468e-bd28-4d9be1819bda
Ankur Moitra → F. Thomson Leighton: https://people.csail.mit.edu/moitra/docs/thesis.pdf Origin: Moitra identifies this as the main unresolved question of his vertex-sparsification work. His 2009 work introduced terminal-only cut sparsifiers, and his joint work with Leighton developed flow sparsifiers; the thesis explicitly asks for constant quality with any terminal-count-only size bound.

**Source.** Ankur Moitra. *Vertex Sparsification and Universal Rounding Algorithms*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2011. Advisor(s): F. Thomson Leighton. [primary source](https://people.csail.mit.edu/moitra/docs/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://toc.csail.mit.edu/user/133) Location: Chapter 7, Open Question 2 and its preceding precise formulation, printed p. 123; flow quality defined in §3.2.

## Q434. The approximate-counting complexity of bipartite independent sets

Attribution: Classical counting and sampling problems restated by Liu, without origin credit. Shared setup for questions 434, 435, 436, 437: An FPRAS estimates Z within relative error ε with probability ≥3/4 in time polynomial in input length and 1/ε. ## Q453. A proof-search procedure competitive with every proof system

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 5

Does a pair (A,P) exist such that, for every pair (B,Q), constants c,C depending only on (B,Q) satisfy time_A(τ)≤C(time_B(τ)+1)^c for every tautology τ? Equivalently, does a p-optimal propositional proof system exist?

**Context.** Attribution: The p-optimal-system problem originates in Krajíček and Pudlák, “Propositional proof systems, the consistency of first order theories and the complexity of computations” (JSL 1989). Shared setup for question 453: A Cook–Reckhow system is a polynomial-time computable map onto the propositional tautologies; a proof is a preimage string. A proof-search pair uses a total deterministic algorithm producing such a proof on every tautology.

**Source.** Jan Krajíček; Pavel Pudlák. *Propositional proof systems, the consistency of first order theories and the complexity of computations*. 1989. [primary source](https://users.math.cas.cz/~pudlak/propositional.pdf) Location: Krajíček–Pudlák1989 §2, especially Theorem 2.1; proof-search formulation in Krajíček2021 Definitions 1.1,2.1 and Theorem 2.4.

**Literature check.** Status, checked 5 October 2026: Egidy’s 2026 primary papers explicitly retain unrelativized TAUT optimality. Their oracle results establish barriers, not a resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02294) · [2](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.88)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 6

## Q523. Length-k walk approximation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

<a id="q541"></a>

## Q541. Exact complexity of finite coupon-collection query scheduling

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

There are n independent dice with known rational outcome probabilities on d colors, each usable once. Choose a permutation before observing outcomes; reveal dice in that order until every color has appeared or all n dice have been revealed. Minimize the expected number of reveals. What is the exact computational complexity of finding an optimal permutation: is there a polynomial-time algorithm in the explicit n×d probability input and its bit length, or is the optimization NP-hard? The prescribed rule keeps revealing if collecting every color has become impossible, until all dice are exhausted.

**Context.** Attribution: The primary September 2026 paper explicitly leaves computational hardness of Finite Coupon Collection open. Its O(log d)-approximation is established, so this question is exact optimization complexity, not existence of that approximation.

**Source.** Evan J. R. Brody; Haya Diwan; Lisa Hellerstein; Thomas Lidbetter. *On Extensions of the Unanimous Vote Problem*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36508) Location: §4 definition and Theorem 7; §6 open questions, printed p. 11.

**Literature check.** Status for question 541, checked 5 October 2026: Read the full 29 September 2026 preprint, including its exact stopping convention, theorem and open questions. Exact-title/finite-coupon follow-up searches through 5 October 2026 found no resolution in the six-day window. This is recent preprint evidence, not a peer-reviewed claim.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Finding an optimal permutation is #P-hard under polynomial-time Turing reductions, already for n=d+1 with all probabilities positive; that restricted search problem is Turing-equivalent to the permanent. No polynomial-time algorithm exists unless every #P function is in FP (hence P=NP). Scope: Turing reductions; no many-one or strong NP-hardness, fixed-colour classification or matching upper classification is claimed. Not independently re-derived. [Details](../../solutions/b4-2026-10-07/B4_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.36508v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.36508)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.1) · **Collection** 6

<a id="q551"></a>

## Q551. Is algebraic proof degree a linear memory lower bound?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

Fix a field K and a constant input-clause width k. For unsatisfiable k-CNFs F, is Deg_PCR,K(F)≤O(Sp_PCR,K(F)), with a constant depending at most on K and k? Here PCR uses Boolean and complementarity axioms, multiplication by a variable and K-linear combinations; Deg minimizes largest monomial degree and Sp minimizes peak total monomial occurrences in retained polynomials, counted with repetition. The current general relation loses a square root; no XOR substitution of F is allowed.

**Context.** Attribution: Authors explicitly ask whether degree lower-bounds monomial space and whether the square-root loss is necessary. Fixed input width is essential to exclude trivial high-degree input axioms; this is not a statement about arbitrary polynomial constraints.

**Source.** Yuval Filmus; Massimo Lauria; Mladen Mikša; Jakob Nordström; Marc Vinyals. *Towards an Understanding of Polynomial Calculus: New Separations and Lower Bounds*. 2025. [primary source](https://mirror.theoryofcomputing.org/articles/v021a004/v021a004.pdf) Location: §9 pp. 36–37; definitions §2 pp. 6–7; §1.3 subsequent developments.

**Literature check.** Status for question 551, checked 5 October 2026: The August 2025 journal version retains the square-root gap. Searches through 2026-10-05 found no general linear comparison. Jaser–Torán TR24-042 does not settle it: its Theorem 30 is retracted in the ECCC author correction. Its claims about XOR-substituted inputs also differ from this no-substitution question.

**Further links.** [1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2024/042/)

<a id="q555"></a>

## Q555. Does generic one-wayness already obstruct threshold-logic proof search?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

Does existence of one-way functions imply that TC^0-Frege is not weakly automatable by deterministic classical algorithms? TC^0-Frege uses constant-depth threshold formulas; weak automatability permits any Cook–Reckhow system with polynomial-time proof translation from TC^0-Frege and a proof-finding algorithm polynomial in its shortest proof length. The hypothesis is generic existence of a standard polynomial-time one-way function, not security of a particular factoring, Diffie–Hellman or lattice construction.

**Context.** Attribution: The authors pose generic-one-way-function hardness for classical computation. Their main quantum LWE result does not resolve it.

**Source.** Noel Arteche; Gaia Carenini; Matthew Gray. *Quantum Automating TC^0-Frege Is LWE-Hard*. 2025. [primary source](https://narteche.github.io/files/papers/quantumatability_oct25.pdf) Location: Introduction, Open problems: Towards generic hardness assumptions, p. 4 (October 2025 full version); journal §1.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 555, checked 5 October 2026: October 2025 manuscript/journal version retains the question. Searches through 2026 found no generic reduction. Its LWE theorem assumes a concrete family and does not imply a statement under bare existence of one-way functions.

**Further links.** [1](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/43528b41-8b32-4e52-9a3d-dfc9670e84e7/content) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.16956v2)


<a id="q561"></a>

## Q561. Exact fixed-degree sum-of-squares refutation for finite-domain constraints

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.5) · **Collection** 6

For every fixed finite rational domain D, finite constraint language Γ and degree d, is the degree-d SoS-CSP decision problem polynomial-time solvable? Given a CSP(Γ) instance in the source’s local polynomial encoding, with equations p_i=0 and domain equations ∏\_{a∈D}(x_j−a)=0, decide whether real polynomials s_l,h_i satisfy −1=Σ\_l s_l²+Σ\_i h_i p_i with all displayed products of degree≤d. Keep the canonical input encoding and exact feasibility. No degree-d refutation does not mean that the CSP is satisfiable.

**Context.** Attribution: Problem 1.5 is explicitly formulated by the authors as the natural fixed-degree SoS algorithm for CSPs. The paper proves polynomial-time solvability for bounded-width constraint languages, leaving the general finite-domain input problem.

**Source.** Alex Bortolotti; Monaldo Mastrolilli; Luis Felipe Vargas. *On the Degree Automatability of Sum-of-Squares Proofs*. 2025. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol334-icalp2025/html/LIPIcs.ICALP.2025.34/LIPIcs.ICALP.2025.34.html) Location: Problem 1.5; Definition 1.1; Corollary 2.2; §9.

**Literature check.** Status for question 561, checked 5 October 2026: ICALP 2025 retains the general problem. Bortolotti–Mastrolilli September 23,2026 extends tractable PC ideal-membership classes and their SoS consequences, but still explicitly calls general SoS degree-automatability open. It does not furnish a decider for all finite-domain SoS-CSP inputs. General polynomial-system coefficient counterexamples cannot simply be transplanted into canonical CSP encodings.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28243)


<a id="q562"></a>

## Q562. Do sparse CNF refutations require unexpectedly large coefficient encodings?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

For unsatisfiable CNFs F with the standard polynomial clause equations and Boolean/complementarity axioms, are minimum monomial size M_P(F) and minimum rational bit complexity B_P(F) polynomially related for P=SOS and P=PCR/Q? In particular, is B_P(F)≤poly(|F|+M_P(F))? Monomial size counts explicit monomials with multiplicity, permitting real coefficients for SOS; bit complexity uses reduced rational fractions in binary and includes PCR inference scalars. Different refutations may attain the two minima. Treat the two systems as one comparison family.

**Context.** Attribution: Hakoniemi explicitly asks whether his exponential separation extends to CNF-derived constraints or whether the measures become polynomially equivalent. His established Q_n counterexample is not a CNF encoding and is excluded.

**Source.** Tuomas Hakoniemi. *Monomial-size vs. Bit-complexity in Sums-of-Squares and Polynomial Calculus*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.07525) Location: §8 Conclusions and open questions, p. 15; definitions §§2.2–2.3 pp. 3–4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 562, checked 5 October 2026: Bortolotti–Mastrolilli–Vargas 2025 §9 explicitly reaffirms this question. September 2026 work supplies further tractable classes, not a universal sparse-CNF bit bound. Searches through 2026-10-05 found no answer. This differs from fixed-degree existence/decision and from monomial working-space lower bounds.

**Further links.** [1](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol334-icalp2025/html/LIPIcs.ICALP.2025.34/LIPIcs.ICALP.2025.34.html) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.28243)

## Q593. Deciding exact probability equivalence of recursive language sensors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

Is there an algorithm deciding, for two finite consistent probabilistic context-free grammars G₁,G₂ over the same finite alphabet with effectively encoded rational rule probabilities, whether P_G₁(w)=P_G₂(w) for every finite word w? Word probability sums all leftmost-derivation probabilities. Keep unrestricted ambiguity; equality of support languages and equality of individual derivation weights are different problems.

## Q594. Deciding the unresolved side of a model-distance threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

For a rational finite labelled Markov chain that terminates almost surely, rational initial laws P,Q, and rational τ∈(0,1), is d_TV(P,Q)≥τ decidable? Here d_TV=sup_E|P(E)−Q(E)| over finite-word events. Equivalently ask d_TV<τ; d_TV>τ is already undecidable. The same ≥ question remains open for infinite-word chains.

**Context.** Attribution: Kiefer poses both questions in §5.

**Source.** Stefan Kiefer. *On Computing the Total Variation Distance of Hidden Markov Models*. 2018. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2018.130) Location: §3.1; §5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 594, 595, checked 5 October 2026: The threshold gap is explicitly reaffirmed in 2022. No resolution of either target located; later infinite-trace metrics differ from total variation.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.06170) · [2](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid%3Ac0162e62-dcda-42ac-958c-db1d5c9fbc3a/files/sx059c8256) · [3](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/120676/7/WRAP-asymmetric-distances-approximate-differential-privacy-Purser-2019.pdf) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18103)


<a id="q595"></a>

## Q595. Polynomial-space approximation for infinite observation-trace distance

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

Can d_TV(P,Q) for rational finite labelled Markov chains generating infinite words be approximated within binary-encoded ε>0 using polynomial workspace in input bit length? P,Q are rational initial laws; events are measurable subsets of infinite traces. Arbitrary-precision computability is known, but the PSPACE bound holds only for finite-word chains.

**Context.** Attribution: Kiefer poses both questions in §5.

**Source.** Stefan Kiefer. *On Computing the Total Variation Distance of Hidden Markov Models*. 2018. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2018.130) Location: §5; Theorem 11.

## Q601. Construct optimal multi-symbol sequence-model compression

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

For a minimal n-state real weighted finite automaton computing an absolutely summable series f:Σ\*→R, |Σ|≥2, and k&lt;n, give a constructive solution of the source’s optimal k-state approximation problem in the Hankel operator norm: minimize ||H_f−H_g||op over series g realizable with at most k states, where H_f(u,v)=f(uv). The unresolved step is constructive noncommutative AAK approximation, not ordinary unconstrained matrix truncation.

**Context.** Doctoral researcher: Clara Lacroce. Dissertation: The approximate minimization problem of weighted finite automata and applications to language modelling: an approach based on Adamyan-Arov-Krein theory. McGill University, 2022. Supervision: Prakash Panangaden (supervisor); Doina Precup (supervisor). Attribution: Joint 2022 paper and Lacroce’s dissertation; the 2021 unary paper, also with Balle and Precup, already requested the extension.

**Source.** Clara Lacroce; Prakash Panangaden; Guillaume Rabusseau. *Towards an AAK Theory Approach to Approximate Minimization in the Multi-Letter Case*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.00172) · [dissertation](https://claralacroce.github.io/static/LacroceClara_PhDthesis.pdf) · [record](https://claralacroce.github.io/static/LacroceClara_PhDthesis.pdf) Location: Paper §1 challenges 1–2; §5. Thesis §9.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 601, checked 5 October 2026: No constructive multi-letter solution located. The 2024/2025 journal result remains unary; ordinary SVD does not ensure a Hankel or nonnegative approximant.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0960129524000276) · [2](https://claralacroce.github.io/publications.html)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

## Q606. Compute linear closures of recursively constrained sensor updates

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

Given a context-free grammar for L⊆Σ\* and rational d×d matrices M_a for a∈Σ, is the linear Zariski closure of {M_{a₁}···M_{a_t}:a₁···a_t∈L} effectively computable? The required output is a finite union of rational vector subspaces, represented by bases, equal to the smallest such finite union containing these matrices. Matrices need not be invertible.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit author-presented computability problem at SAMSA 2026.

**Source.** Yahia-Idriss Benalioua; Nathan Lhote; Pierre-Alain Reynier. *Is the linear Zariski closure of a context-free language of matrices computable?*. 2026. [primary source](https://samsa-workshop.github.io/2026/slides/yahia.pdf) Location: Talk abstract; Questions slides, PDF pp. 33–38.

**Literature check.** Status for question 606, checked 5 October 2026: Explicitly open in 2026; no later resolution located. Ordinary polynomial-ideal Zariski closure and 1-VASS results differ.

**Further links.** [1](https://samsa-workshop.github.io/2026/)


<a id="q608"></a>

## Q608. Compute a transcript within the quasipolynomial sample budget

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

For fixed p∈(0,1), can worst-case exact deletion-trace reconstruction achieve quasipolynomial sample size and runtime, succeeding with probability≥2/3 for every n-bit source? Full reconstruction is required, not pairwise distinguishing.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit runtime gap after the paper’s new sample bound. Shared setup for question 608: A deletion trace of x∈{0,1}^n independently retains each bit with known probability p∈(0,1), deletes the others, and concatenates survivors. Different traces are conditionally independent given x.

**Source.** Arnav Burudgunte; Paul Valiant; Hongao Wang. *Quasipolynomial Trace Reconstruction*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04073) Location: §1, Run time vs. sample complexity; Definition 2; Theorem 43.

**Literature check.** Status for question 608, checked 5 October 2026: July 2026 version checked; no later revision or runtime solution located. Efficient pairwise tests and restricted-string results do not solve full reconstruction.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Rational-to-real retention reduction, now with constant-factor sample overhead. Remaining: partial Recorded from the external B7 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/B7_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B7-R16: submitted proved): A full decoder for each fixed rational retention extends to every fixed real retention through calibration and dyadic thinning, with O(M) traces and T+O(nM polylog(nM)) time for fixed retention/error. Remaining/limits: Validate the actual rational full decoder and its bit-time guarantee. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).


<a id="q609"></a>

## Q609. Polylogarithmic observations for typical exact transcript recovery

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

For every fixed deletion probability δ∈(0,1), can a uniformly random unknown X∈{0,1}^n be reconstructed exactly from (log n)^{Oδ(1)} independent deletion traces, with success probability 1−o_n(1) over X and the channel? No polynomial-time condition is added.

**Context.** Attribution: The paper improves exact average-case reconstruction; the remaining sample gap is reaffirmed in July 2026.

**Source.** Ittai Rubinstein. *Average-Case to (Shifted) Worst-Case Reduction for the Trace Reconstruction Problem*. 2023. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2023.102) Location: §§1–1.1; Theorem 4; 2026 status paper §1.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 609, checked 5 October 2026: July 2026 still reports exp(Õ((log n)^(1/5))) versus Ω̃((log n)^(5/2)). Its worst-case improvement is not established for the shifted oracle. No polylogarithmic solution located.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Hard-pair embedding proves a conditional negative consequence; premise unverified. Remaining: partial Recorded from the external B7 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/B7_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B7-R17: submitted proved): For a hard pair of m-bit words with t-trace TV beta, uniform n-bit reconstruction success is at most [1-2^(-m)(1-beta)]^floor(n/m). Superpolynomially small one-trace TV would rule out every polylogarithmic trace budget. Remaining/limits: Validate the hypothesized superpolynomial one-trace TV theorem before closing Q609. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04073)

## Q610. Accuracy-scalable reconstruction under insertions and deletions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 7

In the source’s insertion–deletion channel R_p, at each step copy the current input bit with probability 1−p, delete it with probability p/2, or insert a uniform random bit with probability p/2 without advancing. For fixed sufficiently small p>0 and uniform S∈{0,1}^n, can its near-linear-time reconstruction theorem be extended to edit error O(εpn), for arbitrary ε>0, using poly(1/ε) traces and success probability at least 1−1/n? Runtime is near-linear in n for fixed p,ε.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit accuracy extension of the three-trace theorem.

**Source.** Diptarka Chakraborty; Debarati Das; Robert Krauthgamer. *Approximate Trace Reconstruction via Median String (In Average-Case)*. 2021. [primary source](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/entities/document/10.4230/LIPIcs.FSTTCS.2021.11) Location: Theorem 1; Conjecture 2; channel R_p.

**Literature check.** Status for question 610, checked 5 October 2026: No resolution located. Later deletion-only few-trace/one-trace results and the Chase–Peres constant-trace existence theorem do not supply this accuracy and runtime guarantee.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B7: submitted unresolved): No new result for insertion/deletion accuracy and runtime. Remaining/limits: unresolved Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.11530) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03292)

## Q611. Reconstruct probability sequences on a fixed finite grid

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

In generalized trace reconstruction, a hidden vector p∈[0,1]^n first emits independent Bernoulli(p_i) bits, then each bit is deleted with fixed probability δ∈(0,1). If all p_i lie in {0,1/k,…,1}, what is the minimax number of traces for recovering p to ℓ∞ error below 1/(2k), with probability at least 2/3? In particular, can superpolynomial-in-n lower bounds persist for a fixed k≥2?

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit finite-grid extension; the displayed accuracy makes exact grid recovery possible by rounding.

**Source.** Joey Rivkin; Gregory Valiant; Paul Valiant. *A Generalized Trace Reconstruction Problem: Recovering a String of Probabilities*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00674) Location: Definition 1; Theorem 2; §1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 611, checked 5 October 2026: No finite-grid resolution located. The July 2026 theorem covers deterministic strings, not fixed k≥2 stochastic emission grids.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Two-point emission reduction, including an interior grid pair; minimax rate remains. Remaining: partial Recorded from the external B7 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/B7_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B7-R18: submitted proved): Binary trace lower bounds transfer to every two-point Bernoulli emission alphabet; for k>=3 the fixed grid admits the interior pair {1/k,2/k}. An unconditional exponential upper bound follows from full-length traces. Remaining/limits: The full minimax rate and an unconditional fixed-grid superpolynomial bound remain. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1145/3717823.3718315) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04073)

## Q613. Sharp decision fidelity from one deleted observation

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

Let A receive n and one δ-deletion trace of x, and output an n-bit estimate. Determine the sharp asymptotic values of L_worst(δ,n)/n and L_avg(δ,n)/n, where L_worst(δ,n)=sup_A inf_x E[LCS(A(trace,n),x)] and L_avg(δ,n)=sup_A E_{X uniform}[LCS(A(trace,n),X)] for fixed δ, already at δ=0.1 or 0.9. Here LCS denotes longest-common-subsequence length, and A is deterministic, as in the source definitions.

**Context.** Attribution: The source groups fixed-noise worst-case and average-case constants as future work.

**Source.** Xi Chen; Anindya De; Chin Ho Lee; Rocco A. Servedio; Sandip Sinha. *Approximate Trace Reconstruction from a Single Trace*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03292) Location: §1.2; §2.2, equations (2), (4).

**Literature check.** Status for question 613, checked 5 October 2026: No sharp fixed-noise frontier located. High-noise and small-noise asymptotics do not settle the stated constants; multiple-trace reconstruction has a different observation budget.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Exact Bayes formula and complete finite computation through n=8; asymptotics remain. Remaining: partial Recorded from the external B7 return  (backfilled 2026-10-08); selected local controls in the intake; external archive not uploaded; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/B7_Research_Report_2026-10-07.md).
- **Related result** (B7-R19: submitted proved): For any fixed prior on length-n words and fixed loss, the one-trace Bayes decoder is independent of retention. Uniform-source optimal LCS is an exact degree-n Bernstein polynomial; all values and decoders through n=8 are computed. Remaining/limits: Fixed-deletion asymptotic constants and the deterministic worst-case frontier remain. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

<a id="q621"></a>

## Q621. Complexity of a self-consistent three-level source ranking

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

For explicitly represented sources in the shared setup, what is the complexity of deciding whether any strongly stable tripartition exists?

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit existence and inference complexity gaps for stable merging. Shared setup for questions 621, 622: For finite nonempty propositional sources S₁,…,S_n, flatten an ordered source partition into classes P₁,…,P_k. A maxcon is consistent M⊆∪P_j whose restriction to every priority prefix is inclusion-maximal consistent there. M induces (R|P|U) according as all, some-but-not-all, or none of each source’s formulas are individually consistent with M. A tripartition is strongly stable if every maxcon induces that same tripartition.

**Source.** Paolo Liberatore. *Belief Integration and Source Reliability Assessment*. 2018. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.1.11238) Location: §8.6; Definitions 1–4, 11.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 621, 622, checked 5 October 2026: No later classification located. Existing two-level and supplied-priority-order results do not settle quantification over self-supporting tripartitions.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

Labels are Y_t=f\*(X_t) for an arbitrary fixed f\*, possibly outside F, and are revealed after prediction.

## Q765. Sharp computational approximation for repeated median testing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

**Context.** Attribution: The conclusion asks both approximation questions. Shared setup for questions 765, 766: Independent nonnegative values V_i have explicitly listed finite-support laws. A total of k oracle tests may be allocated adaptively. A median test reports which half of the current conditional distribution contains V_i; ties use one initial independent uniform tie-breaker per item, reused throughout. <a id="q768"></a>

## Q768. Sharp particle accuracy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

For Algorithm 1, Output Option 1, with multinomial resampling and N particles, what is sharp worst-case terminal TV error in horizon H and N under bounded C and χ²(π\*\_h||πhat_h)≤D at every h, including Vhat=V\*?

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit accuracy, memory and query-complexity questions. Shared setup for questions 768, 769, 770: Known proposal p on A^H and terminal r≥0 with 0&lt;E_p r<∞ define π\*=pr/E_p r and V\*(s)=E_p[r|s]. Vhat=r at depth H. Access permits proposal-transition sampling and prefix-value queries. π\*\_h,πhat_h reweight proposal prefixes by V\*,Vhat. Coverage means V\*(sa)≤CV\*(s) on proposal-supported edges; error means K⁻¹V\*≤Vhat≤KV\*.

**Source.** Noah Golowich; Fan Chen; Dhruv Rohatgi; Raghav Singhal; Carles Domingo-Enrich; Dylan J. Foster; Akshay Krishnamurthy. *Reject, Resample, Repeat: Understanding Parallel Reasoning in Language Model Inference*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.07887) Location: §5; Proposition C.8.

## Q769. Minimum particle memory

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

For binary actions, C=2,K=e³, find minimal worst-case particle cap N(H) giving TV≤1/3. Particles descend from retained predecessors; stage-h laws use only information through h. Within-stage computation is unrestricted. Output must be a retained terminal particle.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit accuracy, memory and query-complexity questions. Shared setup for questions 768, 769, 770: Known proposal p on A^H and terminal r≥0 with 0&lt;E_p r<∞ define π\*=pr/E_p r and V\*(s)=E_p[r|s]. Vhat=r at depth H. Access permits proposal-transition sampling and prefix-value queries. π\*\_h,πhat_h reweight proposal prefixes by V\*,Vhat. Coverage means V\*(sa)≤CV\*(s) on proposal-supported edges; error means K⁻¹V\*≤Vhat≤KV\*.

<a id="q771"></a>

## Q771. Verifier-robust coverage without enumerating the action alphabet

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

Can VGB’s average-error coverage theorem extend to a large action alphabet using only proposal sampling, proposal probability evaluation, and Vhat-prefix queries, without an additional uniform bound on Vhat? For δ∈(0,1) and each depth h assume E_{π\*}[Vhat/V\*]≤1+ε\_V and E_{π\*}[V\*/Vhat]≤1+ε\_V. Seek its coverage guarantee Pr_{Y~π\*}{π\*(Y)/ν(Y)>48H(1+ε\_V)²/δ}≤δ with oracle work polynomial in H,1+ε\_V,δ^(−1) and the true action-coverage C_act, rather than linear in |A|. Unknown terminal rewards are allowed as in Theorem 4.2.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit large-alphabet extension question. Shared setup for question 771: Fix a prompt and finite action alphabet A. A known autoregressive proposal π\_ref on A^H and unknown nonnegative terminal tilt τ with positive finite expectation induce π\*∝π\_ref τ and V\*(prefix)=E_ref[τ|prefix]. Vhat is a nonnegative approximate prefix-value oracle, including at depth H; terminal rewards need not be exact. C_act=max π\*(next action|prefix)/π\_ref(next action|prefix). Each proposal draw, probability evaluation, or Vhat query has unit cost.

**Source.** Dhruv Rohatgi; Abhishek Shetty; Donya Saless; Yuchen Li; Ankur Moitra; Andrej Risteski; Dylan J. Foster. *Taming Imperfect Process Verifiers: A Sampling Perspective on Backtracking*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03149) Location: Remark I.2; Theorem 4.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 771, checked 5 October 2026: No resolution located without additional uniform assumptions.

## Q772. Optimal first-order query complexity of log-concave inference

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

For π∝exp(−V) on R^d with V∈C², I≼∇²V≼κI and ∇V(0)=0, determine the minimax number of adaptive exact queries (V(x),∇V(x)) required to output one sample with TV error≤ε. Randomization and arbitrary computation between queries are allowed. Resolve the joint d,κ,ε dependence, including whether a polynomial dimension factor is necessary at fixed κ. This counts oracle queries, not bit operations or function-evaluation cost hidden inside an oracle.

**Source.** Xiaoyu Chen; Zongchen Chen; Kuikui Liu; Xinyuan Zhang. *Subquadratic Counting via Perfect Marginal Sampling*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02235) Location: §1.1, pp. 3–4; §2.4.

## Q1027. Uniqueness of a one-sided behavioral-error certificate

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

Must Γ₀ have a unique fixed point?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1027: David Purser. On the Complexity of Verifying Differential Privacy. University of Warwick, 2020. Supervision: Andrzej Murawski (supervisor); Dmitry Chistikov (supervisor); Graham Cormode (supervisor). Origin for question 1027: Joint 2019 question; Purser’s Warwick 2020 thesis, Conjecture 5.27, restates it. Supervisors: Andrzej Murawski, Dmitry Chistikov, Graham Cormode. Setup for question 1027: Finite rational LMC: labels ℓ, transitions μ\_s. For α>1 define Λ(a,b)=(a−αb)₊ and K_d(μ,ν)=sup_{f∈[0,1]^S:Λ(f_u,f_v)≤d_uv}Λ(μf,νf). Let Γd_st=1 for unequal labels, otherwise K_d(μ\_s,μ\_t), and d\*=lfpΓ. Modify Γ to return zero wherever d\*=0, obtaining Γ₀.

**Source.** Dmitry Chistikov; Andrzej S. Murawski; David Purser. *Asymmetric Distances for Approximate Differential Privacy*. 2019. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CONCUR.2019.10) · [dissertation](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/153544/1/WRAP_Theses_Purser_2020.pdf) Location: §7; §9.

## Q1180. Infinite word-equation solutions force unbounded powers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

If an unconstrained word equation has infinitely many solutions, must their exponents of periodicity be unbounded?

**Context.** Origin for question 1180: Explicit conjecture; the source discusses its older background. Setup for question 1180: Variables take nonempty words over a finite constant alphabet. A solution’s exponent is the largest k for which u^k, u≠ε, occurs inside some variable-image. Quadratic means each variable occurs at most twice across both sides.

**Source.** Volker Diekert, Silas Natterer, Alexander Thumm. *Quadratic word equations with regular constraints and the exponent of periodicity*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.08208v3) Location: §1.1 and §8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1180, checked 6 October 2026: July 2026 revision still leaves the general case open; quadratic and two-variable cases are known.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.08208v3#S8)

Given finite triangulations of two compact orientable 3-manifolds, is deciding whether they are homeomorphic in NP, with input size the triangulation encoding length?

<a id="q1271"></a>

## Q1271. Linear-time co-lex sorting of deterministic automata

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

Can the maximum co-lex order of a DFA, together with a minimum-size chain partition, be computed in O(m) time, where m is its transition count?

**Context.** Origin for question 1271: Explicit thesis questions. Setup for question 1271: States are reachable from the unique start. Use the thesis’s word-RAM model and ordered integer alphabet with labels fitting O(log m)-bit words. A co-lex order ≤ satisfies, whenever u&lt;v: max λ(u)≤min λ(v); and, for equally labeled edges u′→u and v′→v, u′≤v′. Here λ lists incoming labels, with a least start marker. Wheeler orders are total.

**Source.** Nicola Cotumaccio. *Data Compression Meets Automata Theory*. 2024. Advisor(s): Travis Gagie, Nicola Prezza, Catia Trubiani. [primary source](https://dalspace.library.dal.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/b7cccc15-fc3b-4866-99a6-1debd92f5615/content) Location: Chapter 7, p. 128; §4.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1271, checked 6 October 2026: Reaffirmed in the 2025 thesis survey; later sorting/minimization papers located do not settle these questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://schmiste.github.io/beatcs145.pdf) · [2](https://gssi.it/communication/news-events/item/25426-nicola-cotumaccio-wins-best-italian-phd-thesis-in-theoretical-computer-science?print=1&tmpl=component)

## Q1281. NP membership for mixed-encoding two-counter reachability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

Is exact configuration reachability for these mixed-encoding 2-VASS in NP?

**Context.** Origin for question 1281: Paper question, restated as Open Problem 4 in Sinclair-Banks’s thesis. Setup for question 1281: A 2-VASS has two nonnegative counters and additive integer transitions. Updates to one counter are unary-encoded and those to the other binary-encoded; initial and target counter values are binary-encoded.

**Source.** Filip Mazowiecki, Henry Sinclair-Banks, Karol Węgrzycki. *Coverability in 2-VASS with One Unary Counter is in NP. FoSSaCS 2023*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-30829-1_10) Location: §7, Conclusion and Future Work.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 13

Is coverability NP-hard for 2-VASS with unary updates to one counter, binary updates to the other, and binary initial/target vectors? Coverability requires the target state and componentwise counter values at least the target vector.

**Context.** Origin for question 1282: Explicit thesis question.

**Source.** Henry Sinclair-Banks. *On the complexity of reachability problems in counter systems*. 2024. Advisor(s): Dmitry Chistikov; Marcin Jurdziński. [primary source](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/id/eprint/189757/1/WRAP_Theses_Sinclair-Banks_2024.pdf) Location: Ch. 9, Problem 3, pp. 183–184.

## Q1283. Prime counter languages in every higher dimension

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 13

For every k≥3, does there exist a prime k-counter net?

**Context.** Origin for question 1283: Higher-dimensional existence is explicitly asked in Sinclair-Banks’s thesis Problem 10; the paper conjectures a concrete prime family. Setup for question 1283: A k-counter net is a finite-word automaton with k additive integer counters, initially zero and always nonnegative, without zero tests; acceptance is by final state. It is prime if its language is not a finite intersection of languages recognized using fewer than k counters.

**Source.** Shaull Almagor, Guy Avni, Henry Sinclair-Banks, Asaf Yeshurun. *Dimension-Minimality and Primality of Counter Nets*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.14492) Location: §7, Discussion; compare Conjecture 1 and Sinclair-Banks thesis Open Problem 10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1283, checked 6 October 2026: Dimension two is established in the paper. No higher-dimensional resolution located.

## Q1284. Two-token characterization beyond finite state spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5) · **Collection** 13

Does history-determinism coincide with winning the two-token game for infinite labelled transition systems with parity acceptance? Each round Adam chooses a letter, Eve moves her token, then Adam moves two tokens; Eve must accept whenever either Adam run accepts.

**Context.** Origin for question 1284: Explicit thesis question. Setup for question 1284: History-determinism means resolving nondeterminism from the input prefix so that every accepted infinite word produces an accepting run. Priorities are transition-based; the least infinitely recurring priority is even.

**Source.** Keya Prakash. *History-deterministic parity automata: games, complexity, and the 2-token theorem*. 2025. Advisor(s): Marcin Jurdziński. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12302) Location: Chapter 9, Open Problem 5, pp. 146–147 (arXiv thesis version).

**Literature check.** Status for question 1284, checked 6 October 2026: The July 2026 NP-completeness result is finite-state; no infinite-state characterization resolution located.

<a id="q1372"></a>

## Q1372. Decide first-order definability of regular transductions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.5) · **Collection** 14

Given a deterministic two-way transducer on finite words, is it decidable whether its function is star-free regular?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1372, 1373: question 1372: thesis Open Question 7.5. question 1373: thesis Conjecture 8.46, following Dave–Filiot–Krishna–Lhote (2020). Setup for questions 1372, 1373: For finite words, regular functions use deterministic two-way transducers; star-free means realizable by an aperiodic such transducer. For infinite words, regular functions allow ω-regular lookarounds. Deterministic regular functions omit those lookarounds, use Büchi acceptance, require head positions tending to infinity and infinite output. Continuity is on the function’s domain in Cantor topology.

**Source.** Gaëtan Douéneau-Tabot. *Optimization of string transducers*. 2023. Advisor(s): Olivier Carton and Emmanuel Filiot. [primary source](https://gdoueneau.github.io/pages/DOUENEAU-TABOT_Optimization-transducers_v2.pdf) Location: Open Question 7.5, p. 169; §7.7.2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1372, 1373, checked 6 October 2026: No matching resolution found. The 2024 exact-domain counterexample leaves the explicitly extension-based question 1373 untouched.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CONCUR.2024.21) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.06672)

<a id="q1381"></a>

## Q1381. Characterize compression-compatible polyregular functions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 14

Is every compression-compatible polyregular function rectangular?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1381, 1382: Questions 1–2 in §4; Definition 16. Setup for questions 1381, 1382: Polyregular functions are finite-word functions computed with finitely many nested pebbles. Rectangular functions form the least class containing rational functions, duplication w↦ww, reversal, and rectangle a₁…a_nv↦a₁v…a_nv (disjoint alphabets; other inputs map to ε), closed under composition and separator-delimited listwise map. Compression compatibility means that, for fixed f, an SLP for w can be transformed in polynomial SLP-input time into an SLP for f(w).

**Source.** Mikołaj Bojańczyk and Markus Lohrey. *Transducers on Compressed Strings*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.170) Location: §4, Question 1; Definition 16 and Theorem 17.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1381, 1382, checked 6 October 2026: Both explicitly open in July 2026 proceedings; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol374-icalp2026/html/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.170/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.170.html)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

Is satisfiability of unrestricted word equations in NP?

**Context.** Origin for question 1468: The introduction restates the longstanding NP-membership problem. Setup for question 1468: A word equation U=V uses explicit finite strings of terminal letters and variables over a finite alphabet. A solution substitutes finite words, possibly ε, for variables and fixes each terminal letter.

## Q1469. Decide word equations with linear length constraints

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

Is it decidable whether a word equation has a solution satisfying the supplied length constraints?

**Context.** Origin for question 1469: Source§4 and§6 restate the longstanding word-equations-with-length-constraints problem. Setup for question 1469: A word equation U=V uses explicit finite strings of terminal letters and variables over a finite alphabet. A solution substitutes finite words, possibly ε, for variables and fixes each terminal letter. Length constraints are a finite conjunction of inequalities ∑\_X a_X|h(X)|≤c with integer coefficients and constants.

**Source.** Artur Jeż, Anthony W. Lin, Oliver Markgraf, Philipp Rümmer. *Decision Procedures for Sequence Theories*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-37703-7_2) Location: §4, length-constraint definition; §6.
