## Q1470. Reversal closure for returning translucent automata

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

Is the class of languages recognized by these automata closed under word reversal?

**Context.** Origin for question 1470: The authors restate the older Nagy–Otto reversal question. Setup for question 1470: A returning nondeterministic finite automaton has a state-dependent set of invisible letters. Each step skips invisible letters, deletes the first visible letter, changes state nondeterministically, and restarts at the left end. At the endmarker it may accept, reject, or change state and restart; an accepting computation must halt.

**Source.** Martin Kutrib, Andreas Malcher, Matthias Wendlandt. *On some Open Problems for Finite Automata with Translucent Input Letters*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4204/EPTCS.446.5) Location: §3, paragraph before closure table, pp. 82–83; §2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1470, checked 6 October 2026: Explicitly open after the 2026 paper’s negative reversal results for other variants; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26683)


<a id="q1478"></a>

## Q1478. Context-freeness of primitive binary words

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 8.4) · **Collection** 15

Is the language of primitive binary words context-free?

**Context.** Origin for question 1478: Problems 8.3–8.4 restate older questions; the overlap question is attributed to Berstel in §5.2. Primitive-word context-freeness predates the thesis. Setup for question 1478: Words are finite binary strings. An overlap is axaxa with a a letter and x possibly empty. A nonempty word is primitive if it is not u^k for k≥2.

**Source.** Narad Rampersad. *Overlap-Free Words and Generalizations*. 2007. Advisor(s): Jeffrey Shallit. [primary source](https://cs.uwaterloo.ca/~shallit/narad_phd.pdf) Location: Problem 8.4, p. 92.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1478, checked 6 October 2026: No resolution located. Primitive-word context-freeness is explicitly reaffirmed in Amarilli–Monet–Raphaël–Salvati’s 2026 extended paper.

## Q1567. Unary two-dimensional nonnegative continuous counters

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 16

What are the exact complexities of reachability and coverability for unary integer-update 2-CVASS under Q₊ semantics?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1567, 1568: Two surviving regimes explicitly identified in §6 after the paper’s complexity classification. Setup for questions 1567, 1568: A d-CVASS has finite control and integer update vectors w. A step chooses rational 0<α≤1 and adds αw. Inputs include source p(u) and target q(v); all numerical data, including rational numerators/denominators, use unary encoding. Reachability requires exactly q(v); coverability requires q(v′) with v′≥v. Q₊ semantics prohibits negative counters; Q semantics permits them. Dimension is fixed, not input.

**Source.** Michal Ajdarów, A. R. Balasubramanian, Łukasz Orlikowski. *Reachability in Fixed-Dimensional Continuous VASS. CONCUR 2026, article 7, 24 August 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.CONCUR.2026.7) Location: §6, final paper p. 7:16; §2, Table 1 and Remark 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1567, 1568, checked 6 October 2026: Both remain unresolved in the August 2026 final paper; each problem is in NP. No later solution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30042v1)

<a id="q1572"></a>

## Q1572. Density of tolerant learning degrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.17) · **Collection** 16

Whenever E<\_Learn F, must some G satisfy E<\_Learn G<\_Learn F?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1572, 1573: Two explicit open problems in the paper’s conclusion. Setup for questions 1572, 1573: Fix standard c.e. sets W_e. E is an equivalence relation on indices extending equality of W_e. A family of nonempty c.e. sets is E-learnable if some M:N^{<ω}→N eventually stabilizes, on every enumeration of each W_e in the family, to i E e. No effectivity is imposed unless stated. E≤\_Learn F means every E-learnable family is F-learnable. E₀ identifies finite symmetric differences; E_max identifies all infinite sets, finite nonempty sets with equal maxima, and the empty set with itself.

**Source.** Vittorio Cipriani, Martin Ritter, Luca San Mauro. *The tolerant learning hierarchy for c.e*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06694v1) Location: Question 4.17 and §7(1), pp. 20 and 29.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1572, 1573, checked 6 October 2026: Explicitly open in July 2026; no subsequent resolution located.

E is an equivalence relation on indices extending equality of W_e. A family of nonempty c.e. sets is E-learnable if some M:N^{<ω}→N eventually stabilizes, on every enumeration of each W_e in the family, to i E e. No effectivity is imposed unless stated. E≤\_Learn F means every E-learnable family is F-learnable. E₀ identifies finite symmetric differences; E_max identifies all infinite sets, finite nonempty sets with equal maxima, and the empty set with itself.

## Q1582. Decide simulation between gap-order systems

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 16

Is it decidable whether Duplicator wins the simulation game between two GCS configurations?

**Context.** Origin for question 1582: Simulation was left open by Richard Mayr and Patrick Totzke, Branching-Time Model Checking Gap-Order Constraint Systems (RP 2013). Setup for question 1582: A GCS has finite control and integer-valued variables. Transitions carry action labels and conjunctions x−y≥c, where c≥0 and x,y are current/next variables or integer constants. From designated configurations, Spoiler chooses a transition in the first system; Duplicator must match its label in the second. Duplicator wins infinite play or when Spoiler has no move; failure to match loses.

**Source.** Isa Vialard. *Simulation decidability in Gap-order Constraint Systems*. 2025. [primary source](https://automata.exchange/25.21-simulation-decidability-in-gap-order-constraint-systems/) Location: Problem note final paragraph; Mayr–Totzke §7, with Definitions 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1582, checked 6 October 2026: Reaffirmed by the 2025 author problem note; no later resolution located. Decidable finite-system bisimulation does not settle simulation.

**Further links.** [1](https://cgi.csc.liv.ac.uk/~patrick/assets/pdf/MT2013.pdf)


<a id="q1654"></a>

## Q1654. Improve weighted planar even-cycle transversal

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 17

Does minimum-weight even-cycle transversal in finite planar undirected graphs admit a ρ-approximation for some absolute ρ<47/7? A feasible set meets every even cycle.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1654, 1655: Sun’s Chapter 7, Questions 1 and 4, following the 47/7 ECT algorithm of Göke–Könemann–Mnich–Sun and the 12/5 planar DFVS bound of Berman–Yaroslavtsev. Setup for questions 1654, 1655: Input graphs have nonnegative rational vertex weights; running time is polynomial in the encoded input. A ρ-approximation returns a feasible vertex set of weight at most ρ times optimum.

**Source.** Hao Sun. *Transversal Problems In Sparse Graphs*. 2022. Advisor(s): Jochen Könemann. [primary source](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/items/56923f9c-bd6a-416a-b891-6681872cad6b) Location: Chapter 7, Question 1, p. 77.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1654, 1655, checked 6 October 2026: Through 6 October 2026 no improvement to either general weighted planar ratio was found. Ghorbani–Mnich, ICALP 2026, introduction still identifies 12/5 for planar DFVS; its new 9/4 ratio is for quasi-transitive digraphs and does not settle the planar question. The separate bounded-genus DFVS question has a 2024 constant-factor result and is excluded.

**Further links.** [1](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/bitstreams/9de57206-5e31-4157-adb0-a62532401b26/download) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.04763) · [3](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.96) · [4](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.APPROX/RANDOM.2024.18) · [5](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/items/56923f9c-bd6a-416a-b891-6681872cad6b/full)

<a id="q1678"></a>

## Q1678. Subquadratic synchronizability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 13) · **Collection** 17

Over a fixed alphabet, can synchronizability of an n-state total DFA be decided in O(n^(2−ε)) time for some ε>0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1678, 1679: §8; NC witness question follows Eppstein’s 1990 rank-one-word problem. Synchronizability question also appears in Szykuła (2026). Setup for questions 1678, 1679: A total DFA is given by its transition table. A word induces a zero-one matrix with exactly one 1 per row; its rank equals its image size. NC uses uniform polynomial-size circuits of polylogarithmic depth.

**Source.** Stefan Kiefer; Andrew Ryzhikov. *Spectral and combinatorial methods for efficiently computing the rank of unambiguous finite automata*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09703v4) Location: §8, third bullet; Szykuła Problem 13.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1678, 1679, checked 6 October 2026: September 24 v4 and September 25 journal version explicitly retain both; finding a matrix is distinct from computing its minimum possible rank.

**Further links.** [1](https://lmcs.episciences.org/19531) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24245)

<a id="q1680"></a>

## Q1680. Unambiguous completeness complexity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

What is the exact complexity of deciding completeness for a given unambiguous NFA?

**Context.** Origin for question 1680: §9. Lower bounds are proved even for strongly connected inputs. Setup for question 1680: NFAs are finite labelled transition systems without designated initial/final states. Unambiguous means at most one path with any given label between any fixed ordered pair of states. Complete means every finite word labels at least one path. Mortal means it labels none.

**Source.** Stefan Kiefer; Andrew Ryzhikov. *The Complexity of Reachability Problems in Strongly Connected Finite Automata*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.MFCS.2025.62) Location: §9, first paragraph, p. 62:16.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1680, checked 6 October 2026: Completeness remains between NL-hardness and DET membership; September 2026 rank algorithms do not close that gap.

**Further links.** [1](https://drops.dagstuhl.de/storage/00lipics/lipics-vol345-mfcs2025/LIPIcs.MFCS.2025.62/LIPIcs.MFCS.2025.62.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09703v4)

## Q1681. Rational conjugacy complexity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

What is the exact computational complexity of deciding whether every (u,v)∈R consists of conjugate words?

**Context.** Origin for question 1681: The exact-complexity question is explicit in the revised primary paper, unlike the less quantified Autobóz direct-algorithm prompt. Setup for question 1681: Input is a finite rational expression for R⊆Σ\*×Σ\*, using finite pairs of words, union, componentwise concatenation, and star. Words u,v are conjugate when u=xy and v=yx for some words x,y.

**Source.** C. Aiswarya; Amaldev Manuel; Saina Sunny. *Deciding Conjugacy of a Rational Relation*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.06777v4) Location: §9, Conclusion, p. 50.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1681, checked 6 October 2026: July 7 v4 retains the exponential-time upper bound and unresolved exact complexity; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://automata.exchange/25.23-complexity-of-deciding-conjugacy-of-rational-relations/)


<a id="q1682"></a>

## Q1682. Recognizing Wheeler transducers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

Is membership of a given subsequential transducer in this Wheeler class decidable?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1682, 1683: Definitions 4–5 and §4 of corrected v3; the original definition was replaced. Setup for questions 1682, 1683: Transducers are trimmed one-way deterministic subsequential machines, with ordered finite alphabets, word outputs and final outputs. Put Iq={u:δ(s,u)=q}. Wheeler means the state order induced by colex-separated Iq is total and u↦ω(s,u) is piecewise monotone on each Iq: finitely many convex pieces, each nondecreasing or nonincreasing in colex order.

**Source.** Giovanna D’Agostino; Andrea Paradiso. *Finite-State Transducers in the Wheeler Setting*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29405v3) Location: §4, p. 16; Definitions 4–5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1682, 1683, checked 6 October 2026: August 13 v3 explicitly retains both decision questions; no later solution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.29405v3)

<a id="q2477"></a>

## Q2477. Optimal proof systems beyond NP

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Does any language L∉NP admit a length-optimal Cook–Reckhow proof system?

**Context.** Origin for question 2477: Sadowski 1997; Köbler–Messner 1998; Messner 2000; restated §1. Setup for question 2477: A proof system for L⊆{0,1}\* is a total polynomial-time map with range L. It is length-optimal if it simulates every such system via a polynomially length-bounded translation, with no computability requirement on that translation.

**Source.** Fabian Egidy. *Recursive Jump Operators and Optimal Proof Systems*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2026.88) Location: §1, p. 88:2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2477, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.


<a id="q2478"></a>

## Q2478. Polynomial calculus beats clause space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Over some fixed field, is there a family of unsatisfiable CNFs F_n with PCR monomial space o(Sp_Res(F_n)), where Sp_Res is minimum resolution clause space?

**Context.** Origin for question 2478: ICALP 2013; final §9. Setup for question 2478: Encode clauses by products of literal polynomials, with true=0 and Boolean axioms x²−x=0; PCR also has complementary variables x̄ with x+x̄−1=0. PC/PCR refutations download axioms, take linear combinations, multiply by variables, and erase until deriving 1. Space is peak simultaneous monomial occurrences, counting repetitions; size counts all occurrences; degree is maximal monomial degree. Resolution space counts simultaneous clauses.

**Source.** Yuval Filmus; Massimo Lauria; Mladen Mikša; Jakob Nordström; Marc Vinyals. *Towards an Understanding of Polynomial Calculus: New Separations and Lower Bounds*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4086/toc.2025.v021a004) Location: §9, p. 37, final paragraph.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2478, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.


<a id="q2479"></a>

## Q2479. Unprovable fixed circuit bounds in S₂¹

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.1) · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

For every integer k≥1, is S₂¹ unable to prove P⊆SIZE[n^k]?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2479, 2480: Problems 5.1,5.2,5.5; earlier bounded-arithmetic program. Setup for questions 2479, 2480: S₂¹ and T₂ⁱ are Buss bounded-arithmetic theories: polynomial induction for Σᵇ₁ and full induction for Σᵇᵢ, respectively. SIZE[n^k] uses nonuniform fan-in-two Boolean circuits of at most n^k gates. Class-inclusion provability means the standard circuit-upper-bound sentence for every appropriately coded language.

**Source.** Igor C. Oliveira. *Meta-Mathematics of Computational Complexity Theory*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04416v1) Location: Open Problem 5.1, p. 16.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2479, 2480, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026: question retained in the reviewed source; targeted searches found no matching resolution.

<a id="q2584"></a>

## Q2584. Linear-query provenance minimization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 7.8.9) · **Collection** 26

For every such fixed Q, can minFACT(Q,D) be computed in time polynomial in |D|?

**Context.** Origin for question 2584: Thesis Conjecture 7.8.9, developed from work with Wolfgang Gatterbauer. Setup for question 2584: Fix a self-join-free Boolean conjunctive query Q whose atoms admit a path ordering in which each variable occurs consecutively. For a finite database D, its provenance is the monotone DNF having one tuple-variable product for each satisfying witness. minFACT(Q,D) is the least number of variable occurrences in an equivalent monotone AND/OR formula.

**Source.** Neha Makhija. *Reverse Query Optimization*. 2025. Advisor(s): Wolfgang Gatterbauer. [primary source](https://people.cs.umass.edu/~nehamakhija/files/2025-Makhija_Reverse_Query_Optimization.pdf) Location: §7.8.3, Conjecture 7.8.9, printed p. 119; definitions §7.2, p. 80.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2584, checked 7 October 2026: No resolution located in the bounded 7 October 2026 search.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.14307) · [2](https://nehamakhija.github.io/)

## Q2587. Identity padding and CNOT distance

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 26

For all 1≤b≤a and M∈GL(b,F₂), is δ\_a(M⊕I_{a−b})=δ\_b(M)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2587, 2588: The embedding conjecture is the authors’; the diameter question originates with Marc Bataille. Setup for questions 2587, 2588: For M∈GL(n,F₂), let δ\_n(M) be the least number of transvections I+E_ij, i≠j, whose product is M; every ordered pair of wires is available and no other gates are allowed.

**Source.** Jens Emil Christensen; Søren Fuglede Jørgensen; Andreas Pavlogiannis; Jaco van de Pol. *On Exact Sizes of Minimal CNOT Circuits*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01467) Location: §4.2, Conjecture 2, arXiv p. 13.

## Q2596. Optimal marked-grid rendezvous time

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 26

Determine the optimal asymptotic worst-case rendezvous time, measured from the later wake-up, with a meeting before that wake-up assigned time zero?

**Context.** Origin for question 2596: Authors’ concluding open problem. Setup for question 2596: Two anonymous agents on the oriented square grid Z² run the same deterministic algorithm with full memory. Each round they stay or traverse one edge. Their starting nodes are marked initially; every first visit permanently adds the same indistinguishable mark, detectable on arrival. An adversary chooses starting nodes at distance at most D and arbitrary wake-up delay. The agents know neither D nor the delay; edge-crossings are not meetings.

**Source.** Younan Gao; Andrzej Pelc. *Sniffing helps to meet: Deterministic rendezvous of anonymous agents in the grid*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tcs.2025.115395) Location: ArXiv §7, pp. 25–26; model §1.1; journal Conclusion.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2596, checked 7 October 2026: The journal retains the gap between D^√2 and D²; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14969)


<a id="q2714"></a>

## Q2714. Diophantine decidability in Thompson V

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.1) · **Collection** 28

Is there an algorithm deciding whether a finite system of equations over V has a solution?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2714, 2715: Authors’ Questions 4.1 and 4.3. Setup for questions 2714, 2715: F is the group of orientation-preserving piecewise-linear homeomorphisms of [0,1] with dyadic breakpoints and slopes powers of 2. V consists of homeomorphisms of {0,1}^N induced by finite binary-prefix replacements. Equations have coefficients represented by words in fixed finite generators; variables range over the group.

**Source.** Luna Elliott and Alex Levine. *The Diophantine problem in Thompson’s group F*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14970) Location: Question 4.1, p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2714, 2715, checked 7 October 2026: The paper proves systems undecidable in F and T, not these targets; Elliott’s June 2026 list retains them. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1090/mcom/4113) · [2](https://le27.github.io/L-Elliott/questions/)

## Q2776. Quantum-matching decidability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Is there an algorithm deciding, from G, whether such a finite-dimensional quantum perfect matching exists?

**Context.** Origin for question 2776: Thesis §1.1.2; graph-decidability question retained in the joint paper. Setup for question 2776: For a finite simple graph G, a finite-dimensional quantum perfect matching is a dimension d≥1 and matrices P_e=P_e\*=P_e²∈M_d(C), one per edge, with Σ\_{e∋v}P_e=I_d for every vertex v. No commutation between disjoint edges is required.

**Source.** Seyed Sajjad Nezhadi. *Quantum Games, Graphs, and Gödel*. University of Maryland, 2025. Advisor(s): Matthew Coudron. [primary source](https://sajjad-nezhadi.github.io/pdfs/Thesis.pdf) Location: Paper §1.2; Lemma 6.1; thesis §1.1.2, p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2776, checked 7 October 2026: The current joint paper leaves graph decidability open; hypergraph undecidability does not settle it.

<a id="q2780"></a>

## Q2780. Decidability of branching-VASS reachability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

With d part of the input, is it decidable whether a specified configuration is the root of a run?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2780, 2781: General reachability reaffirmed in the 2026 paper; exact 2-BVASS complexity explicitly left open by the 2025 predecessor. Setup for questions 2780, 2781: A d-BVASS has finite states Q and finitely many rules (p₁,…,p_k,a,q), with a∈Z^d and k≥0. A run is a finite tree labelled q(x), x∈N^d: a node with children pᵢ(xᵢ) satisfies x=a+Σᵢxᵢ for a rule. Nullary rules provide leaves. All rules, states and target configurations are explicit; integers are binary encoded.

**Source.** Clotilde Bizière; Jérôme Leroux; Grégoire Sutre. *Bridging the Gap Between Plain VASS and Branching VASS*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15869) Location: §1 and §8; model §2.3.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2780, 2781, checked 7 October 2026: September 2026 version still leaves general reachability and two-dimensional exact complexity unresolved.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.MFCS.2025.22)

## Q2782. Decidability of the nondeterministic tree index

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Given such an automaton A and a finite priority interval J, is it decidable whether L(A) is recognized by a nondeterministic parity tree automaton using only priorities in J?

**Context.** Origin for question 2782: Explicit open nondeterministic Rabin–Mostowski index problem in the introduction. Setup for question 2782: A nondeterministic parity automaton on infinite full binary Σ-labelled trees has finite states and transitions (q,a,q₀,q₁) with natural-number priorities. A run accepts when, on every branch, the largest priority occurring infinitely often is even.

**Source.** Olivier Idir; Karoliina Lehtinen. *Using Games and Universal Trees to Characterise Nondeterministic Index of Tree Languages*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.ICALP.2025.160) Location: Introduction and §2.1; index problem definition.

<a id="q2783"></a>

## Q2783. Sequential two-way realization of monotone functions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 28

Can every prefix-monotone regular partial function be realized by a sequential two-way transducer?

**Context.** Origin for question 2783: Explicit author-contributed problem following the doctoral-first search. Setup for question 2783: A regular partial function f:Σ\*⇀Γ\* is realized by a deterministic two-way finite-state transducer with read-only input ⊢w⊣ and append-only output; accepting runs end at ⊣. Prefix-monotone means u,v∈dom(f) and u≼v imply f(u)≼f(v). A sequential two-way transducer permits no transition reading ⊣, hence stops on its first arrival there.

**Source.** Nathan Lhote. *Monotone Regular Functions*. 2024. [primary source](https://automata.exchange/24.17-monotone-regular-functions/) Location: Autobóz 24.17, final question.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2783, checked 7 October 2026: Author problem retained; bounded searches found no resolution through 7 October 2026.


<a id="q2852"></a>

## Q2852. SFVS on directed-path graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.1.5) · **Collection** 29

Determine the complexity of weighted and unweighted SFVS on directed-path graphs.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2852, 2853, 2854: Ioannina PhD; advisor Charis Papadopoulos. Origin for questions 2852, 2853, 2854: Questions from Tzimas–Papadopoulos research, collected in the thesis. Setup for questions 2852, 2853, 2854: Graphs are finite, simple and undirected. Given S⊆V(G), SFVS finds minimum-cardinality X⊆V(G) such that G−X has no cycle meeting S. Weighted SFVS minimizes the sum of binary-encoded positive integer vertex weights. Directed-path graphs are intersection graphs of directed paths in an oriented tree. Leafage ℓ minimizes the leaves of a subtree-intersection host tree. A split graph has a clique/independent-set partition. Strongly chordal means chordal with an odd chord in every even cycle of length≥6.

**Source.** Spyridon Tzimas. *Path and Cycle Hitting Problems: Algorithms and Complexity*. University of Ioannina, 2024. Advisor(s): Charis Papadopoulos. [primary source](https://stzimas.github.io/files/STzimas_PhD_Thesis.pdf) Location: Question 9.1.5, p. 128.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2852, 2853, 2854, checked 7 October 2026: The 2024 thesis leaves these open; no later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://stzimas.github.io/) · [2](https://www.cs.uoi.gr/~charis/students.html) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00453-023-01149-5) · [4](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ic.2026.105463)

## Q2869. Partial versus complete automaton semigroups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.1.0.1) · **Collection** 29

Is every S(T) isomorphic to S(U) for some finite complete deterministic letter-to-letter transducer U?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2869, 2870, 2871: Earlier structural and group questions repeated in the thesis. Setup for questions 2869, 2870, 2871: A finite deterministic letter-to-letter transducer T=(Q,Σ,δ) may be partial. All state-induced partial maps on Σ\* generate S(T) under nonempty composition. Complete means each state/input has a transition. For complete invertible T, G(T) includes the state maps’ inverses.

**Source.** Jan Philipp Wächter. *Automaton Structures – Decision Problems and Structure Theory*. University of Stuttgart, 2020. Advisor(s): Volker Diekert. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1226763316/34) Location: Open Problem 1.1.0.1, p. 21.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2869, 2870, 2871, checked 7 October 2026: Group problems reaffirmed in 2026; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://jan-philipp-waechter.bitbucket.io/slides/Liverpool2026_handout.pdf) · [2](https://jan-philipp-waechter.bitbucket.io/)

## Q2875. Exact complexity for deterministic limit Parikh automata

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 29

Are universality and language inclusion Π₂^P-complete?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2875, 2876: Mario Grobler. Counting and Sliding. Verifying and Restoring Healthy Systems. PhD, University of Bremen, 2024. Advisors: Sebastian Siebertz. Origin for questions 2875, 2876: Joint-paper questions repeated in Grobler’s dissertation. Setup for questions 2875, 2876: A deterministic limit Parikh automaton has finite control, exactly one transition per state/letter adding a vector in N^d, initial state, Büchi states F, and a semilinear C⊆(N∪{∞})^d. It accepts when F recurs infinitely and the coordinatewise limit sum lies in C. Dimension is input; constants are binary, and C is an explicit finite union of base-plus-period sets, allowing ∞ with 0·∞=0.

**Source.** Mario Grobler; Sebastian Siebertz. *Deterministic Parikh automata on infinite words*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14737v2) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 20; thesis p. 91.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2875, 2876, checked 7 October 2026: Latest version checked; no later resolution found in bounded searches.

<a id="q2878"></a>

## Q2878. DA reachability closure

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 38) · **Collection** 29

For immediate-observation protocols, does forward reachability preserve FO²[<]-definable languages of configurations?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2878, 2879: Explicit primary-paper conjectures. Setup for questions 2878, 2879: Configurations are nonempty words over finite Q. A rule changes two distinct positions, testing either their order or true; states have Boolean outputs. No-ops are allowed. Fairness requires every successor of any infinitely recurring configuration to recur infinitely. A decider stabilizes unanimously to the same answer on every fair run from each input word over Σ⊆Q. PP[<] is its language class. Immediate-observation rules change at most one position. A poPA is a letter-reading NFA whose transitions respect a state partial order, accepting by final state and a Presburger constraint on transition counts; weak unambiguity means at most one accepting run.

**Source.** Michael Blondin; Michaël Cadilhac; Benjamin Courchesne; Lucie Guillou; Corto Mascle; Isa Vialard. *Population Protocols over Ordered Agents*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09937v1) Location: Conjecture 38, §7.2, p. 167:18.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2878, 2879, checked 7 October 2026: July 2026 conjectures; bounded later searches found no resolution.

## Q2881. Bireversibility forced by free first-level-transitive actions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.16) · **Collection** 29

If reduced invertible T generates a nonabelian free group acting transitively on Σ, must its dual have a bireversible component?

**Context.** Origin for question 2881: Explicit question in the current revised paper. Setup for question 2881: A finite complete Mealy automaton T=(Q,Σ,δ,λ) is invertible when λ(q,−) permutes Σ; reduced means distinct states induce distinct tree automorphisms. Its state maps generate G(T). Reversible means δ(−,a) permutes Q for each a. Its inverse swaps input/output labels; bireversible means both T and its inverse are reversible. The dual swaps states with letters: q —a/b→r becomes a —q/r→b. A dual component is the induced automaton on a first-level G(T)-orbit.

**Source.** Daniele D’Angeli; Emanuele Rodaro. *Freeness Obstructions for Self-Similar Groups Acting on Rooted Trees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11048v4) Location: Problem 6.16, p. 24; definitions §2.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2881, checked 7 October 2026: Latest version checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.


<a id="q2882"></a>

## Q2882. Parikh-implementable history-deterministic resolvers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

Does every history-deterministic Parikh automaton admit a DPA-implementable resolver?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2882: see question 2875. Origin for question 2882: Explicit journal-paper question. Setup for question 2882: A finite-word Parikh automaton has letter-reading transitions adding vectors in N^d, and accepts by final state and a semilinear constraint on the total. A resolver selects the next transition from the transition-run prefix ρ and next letter a, and must accept every word in the automaton’s language. It is DPA-implementable if, for each a and transition δ, {ρ:t(ρ,a)=δ} is recognized by a deterministic Parikh automaton over the transition alphabet.

**Source.** Enzo Erlich; Mario Grobler; Shibashis Guha; Ismaël Jecker; Karoliina Lehtinen; Martin Zimmermann. *History-deterministic Parikh Automata*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.07745v4) Location: §7, pp. 26–27; conclusion §8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2882, checked 7 October 2026: Latest journal version checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://media.suub.uni-bremen.de/handle/elib/8265)

## Q2883. Complementing weakly-unambiguous Parikh automata

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

Are languages of weakly-unambiguous finite-word Parikh automata closed under complement?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2883: Florent Koechlin. Systèmes de fonctions holonomes, application à la théorie des automates. PhD, Université Gustave Eiffel, 2021. Advisors: Arnaud Carayol; Cyril Nicaud. Origin for question 2883: Primary-paper question also discussed in Koechlin’s dissertation. Setup for question 2883: A Parikh automaton adds nonnegative integer vectors along transitions and accepts by final control state and a semilinear total-vector constraint. Weak unambiguity requires at most one accepting run per word; rejecting runs may be multiple. Finite-state transitions read letters.

**Source.** Alin Bostan; Arnaud Carayol; Florent Koechlin; Cyril Nicaud. *Weakly-unambiguous Parikh automata and their link to holonomic series*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09823v1) Location: §3.2, p. 7.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2883, checked 7 October 2026: Finite-state question reaffirmed July 2026; no later resolution found.

<a id="q2884"></a>

## Q2884. Exponential determinization after one random transition

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

For every such fixed alphabet and sequence (fₙ), do constants c,ε>0 exist with Pr[Dₙ≥2^{cn}]≥ε for all sufficiently large n?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2884: see question 2883. Origin for question 2884: Explicit journal-paper strengthening question. Setup for question 2884: Fix finite Σ, |Σ|≥2, and a∈Σ. Independently choose each transition target of a complete n-state DFA, an initial state, and p,q uniformly in [n]; add an a-transition p→q, allowing coincidence with the old one. Independently mark each state final with probability fₙ∈(0,1), where min(fₙ,1−fₙ)≥α/√n eventually for some α>0. Let Dₙ be the minimal equivalent DFA’s size.

**Source.** Arnaud Carayol; Philippe Duchon; Florent Koechlin; Cyril Nicaud. *Random Deterministic Automata With One Added Transition*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06591v4) Location: §11, p. 11:31; model §2, Theorem 3.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2884, checked 7 October 2026: Current full journal paper checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

<a id="q2960"></a>

## Q2960. Width-parameterized MCS extension

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Can MCS-PSOP be solved in f(w)n^O(1) time for some computable f?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2960, 2961: Author’s algorithmic questions. Setup for questions 2960, 2961: PSOP asks whether a search order extends a given partial order on V(G). Graphs are finite, simple and connected, with n vertices; starts and ties are unrestricted. MCS repeatedly selects a vertex with most visited neighbours. LBFS starts with empty labels, repeatedly chooses a lexicographically largest label, and appends n−i to unvisited neighbours after step i. Width w is maximum antichain size.

**Source.** Robert Scheffler. *The Partial Search Order Problem*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.37236/12654) Location: §7, p. 34.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2960, 2961, checked 7 October 2026: Checked 7 October 2026; no later resolution located in targeted searches.

## Q2971. Generalized BFS-tree recognition on chordal graphs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 30

Given a chordal graph G and a spanning tree T⊆G, determine the complexity of deciding whether some BFS order (v₁,…,v_n) makes T[{v₁,…,v_i}] connected for every 1≤i≤n.

**Context.** Origin for question 2971: Authors’ remaining recognition case. Setup for question 2971: Graphs are finite, connected, simple and undirected. A BFS order starts arbitrarily; afterwards it selects an unvisited vertex whose earliest visited neighbour occurs earliest, breaking ties arbitrarily. Vertices with no visited neighbour are ineligible while another choice exists. Chordal means no induced cycle of length≥4.

**Source.** Florian Krowiorz; Robert Scheffler. *Generalized Graph Search Trees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08625) Location: §6, p. 21; Problem 1 and Lemma 3.2, pp. 5–6.

## Q2986. Full orbit verification with a Positivity oracle

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Is acceptance of this characteristic word decidable by a Turing machine with the Positivity oracle, without assuming M diagonalisable?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2986: see question 2978. Origin for question 2986: Explicit primary-paper question. Setup for question 2986: Input consists of a matrix M, vector s and finitely many semialgebraic predicates T_i, all with real-algebraic entries or coefficients, plus a deterministic Muller automaton. At time n≥0 it reads which T_i contain M^n s; acceptance is determined by the set of infinitely recurring states. Algebraic numbers have polynomial/isolation encodings. A Positivity oracle decides nonnegativity of every term of an arbitrary integer linear recurrence sequence.

**Source.** Toghrul Karimov; Edon Kelmendi; Joris Nieuwveld; Joël Ouaknine; James Worrell. *The Power of Positivity*. 2023. [primary source](https://toghrul-karimov.github.io/assets/papers/positivity.pdf) Location: §VI, PDF p. 9, question (i).

**Literature check.** Status for question 2986, checked 7 October 2026: Current source checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.graduateschool-computerscience.de/doctoral-students/phd-defenses/phd-defenses-2024/)

<a id="q2988"></a>

## Q2988. Recognizing prophetic rational functions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 8.25) · **Collection** 30

Given a rational function, is it decidable whether some prophetic transducer computes exactly it?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2988, 2989: Explicit dissertation class-membership conjectures. Setup for questions 2988, 2989: Functions are partial A^ω→B^ω for finite alphabets. Rational functions are specified by functional nondeterministic one-way Büchi transducers allowing ε-input moves, with finite-word transition outputs and infinite accepted output. A transducer is prophetic if each input has at most one Büchi-final run across all starting states. Regular functions are specified by deterministic two-way Büchi transducers with ω-regular lookarounds; accepting runs tend to∞ and output infinitely many letters.

**Source.** Gaëtan Douéneau-Tabot. *Optimization of string transducers*. Université Paris Cité, 2023. Advisor(s): Olivier Carton; Emmanuel Filiot. [primary source](https://gdoueneau.github.io/pages/DOUENEAU-TABOT_Optimization-transducers_v2.pdf) Location: Conjecture 8.25, p. 200.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2988, 2989, checked 7 October 2026: Current source checked; bounded later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://gdoueneau.github.io/)

## Q3075. Finite regular colourability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture VIII.6.1) · **Collection** 31

Is deciding whether an automatic graph has a finite regular colouring recursively enumerable complete?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3075, 3076, 3077: Thesis conjectures; the clique question repeats Barceló–Figueira–Morvan (2023). Setup for questions 3075, 3076, 3077: An automatic directed graph has a regular language of finite-word vertices and a synchronously regular, padded edge relation. A proper colouring separates the endpoints of every edge; it is regular if its finitely many colour classes are regular languages. Let K_m have m vertices, with both arcs between each distinct pair and no loops.

**Source.** Rémi Morvan. *Homomorphism Problems in Graph Databases and Automatic Structures*. 2025. Advisor(s): Diego Figueira, Nathanaël Fijalkow. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07422) Location: Conjecture VIII.6.1, p. 290.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3075, 3076, 3077, checked 8 October 2026: Thesis and 2026 follow-on checked; no matching resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18238) · [2](https://www.morvan.xyz/phd/)

<a id="q3079"></a>

## Q3079. Semilinear diagonal predecessors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 25.8) · **Collection** 31

For every 2-BVASS, source state q and fixed target configuration t, is {x∈N² : a run has all leaves (q,x) and root t} semilinear?

**Context.** Origin for question 3079: Original doctoral-student problem page; no completed degree or unverified advisor is asserted. Setup for question 3079: A 2-BVASS has finite control and counters in N². Unary steps add fixed integer vectors without leaving N². A branching rule (p,q,r) combines children (p,x),(q,y) into (r,x+y). Runs are finite trees; every leaf carries the same source configuration. A set is semilinear if it is a finite union of b+Σ\_i Np_i.

**Source.** Clotilde Bizière. *Semilinearity of 2-BVASS backward reachability set (Autobóz Problem 25.8)*. 2025. [primary source](https://automata.exchange/25.8-semilinearity-of-2-bvass-backward-reachability-set/) Location: Problem 25.8, final question and run definition.

**Literature check.** Status for question 3079, checked 8 October 2026: July 2026 decidability claim concerns reachability, not semilinearity of this diagonal predecessor set.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09558)
